“Yes;” interposed Dr. Gresham, laughing heartily at Dr. Latrobe’s discomfiture. “He belongs to that negro race both by blood and choice. His father’s mother made overtures to receive him as her grandson and heir, but he has nobly refused to forsake his mother’s people and has cast his lot with them.”

“And I,” said Dr. Latimer, “would have despised myself if I had done otherwise.”

===Chinese===
“这几天你一直在医学大会上同我交往；我看不出这样的接触把你往下拖了——拖了吗？”

“你！这同跟黑鬼打交道有什么相干？”拉特罗布医生生硬地问道。

“那个种族的血液，正在我的血管里奔流。我就是他们中的一员。”拉蒂默医生答道，骄傲地昂起了头。

“你！”拉特罗布医生惊呼起来，满面涨红，一副深感错愕的神情。

“正是；”格雷沙姆医生插话道，眼见拉特罗布医生这般窘迫，不由得开怀大笑。“论血统、论自己的抉择，他都属于那个黑人种族。他的祖母曾主动提出认他做孙子和继承人，但他高尚地拒绝背弃他母亲的族人，把自己的命运同他们连在了一起。”

“而我呢，”拉蒂默医生说，“若是做了别的选择，我会瞧不起自己。”

===Original===
“Well, well,” said Dr. Latrobe, rising, “I was never so deceived before. Good morning!”

Dr. Latrobe had thought he was clear-sighted enough to detect the presence of negro blood when all physical traces had disappeared. But he had associated with Dr. Latimer for several days, and admired his talent, without suspecting for one moment his racial connection. He could not help feeling a sense of vexation at the signal mistake he had made.

Dr. Frank Latimer was the natural grandson of a Southern lady, in whose family his mother had been a slave. The blood of a proud aristocratic ancestry was flowing through his veins, and generations of blood admixture had effaced all trace of his negro lineage. His complexion was blonde, his eye bright and piercing, his lips firm and well moulded; his manner very affable; his intellect active and well stored with information. He was a man capable of winning in life through his rich gifts of inheritance and acquirements. When freedom came, his mother, like Hagar of old, went out into the wide world to seek a living for herself and child. Through years of poverty she labored to educate her child, and saw the glad fruition of her hopes when her son graduated as an M.D. from the University of P﻿—﻿—.

After his graduation he met his father’s mother, who recognized him by his resemblance to her dear, departed son. All the mother love in her lonely heart awoke, and she was willing to overlook “the missing link of matrimony,” and adopt him as her heir, if he would ignore his identity with the colored race.

Before him loomed all the possibilities which only birth and blood can give a white man in our Democratic country. But he was a man of too much sterling worth of character to be willing to forsake his mother’s race for the richest advantages his grandmother could bestow.

Dr. Gresham had met Dr. Latimer at the beginning of the convention, and had been attracted to him by his frank and genial manner. One morning, when conversing with him, Dr. Gresham had learned some of the salient points of his history, which, instead of repelling him, had only deepened his admiration for the young doctor. He was much amused when he saw the pleasant acquaintanceship between him and Dr. Latrobe, but they agreed to be silent about his racial connection until the time came when they were ready to divulge it; and they were hugely delighted at his signal blunder.

===Chinese===
“好吧，好吧，”拉特罗布医生说着，站起身来，“我从没这样看走眼过。早安！”

拉特罗布医生原以为，自己的眼力足够犀利，即便体貌上的痕迹已经消磨殆尽，也能察觉出黑人血液的存在。可他同拉蒂默医生交往了好几天，赏识他的才华，却一刻也不曾疑心到他的种族渊源。想到自己犯下的这等重大失误，他禁不住生出几分懊恼。

弗兰克·拉蒂默医生（Frank Latimer）是南方一位太太的私生孙，他的母亲曾是那位太太家的奴隶。高傲的贵族先人的血液，在他血管里流淌；几代人的血胤混融，已把他黑人血脉的一切痕迹抹除净尽。他肤色白皙，眼睛明亮而锐利，嘴唇坚定而轮廓姣好；举止十分谦和；头脑活跃，学识丰富。凭着他得自继承与修习的丰厚天赋，他是个能在人生中赢得成功的人。自由到来之时，他母亲像古时的夏甲（Hagar）一样，走进茫茫人世，为自己和孩子谋一条生路。她在穷困中操劳多年，供儿子读书受教育，终于欣喜地看到自己的期望开花结果——她的儿子以医学博士（M.D.）的身份，从 P—— 大学毕了业。

毕业之后，他见到了父亲的母亲；老人从他身上认出了她那亲爱的亡故儿子的模样。她孤寂心中全部的母爱都苏醒了。只要他肯抹去自己同有色人种的身份关联，她情愿不计“婚姻上缺失的那一环”，收他做自己的继承人。

在我们这个民主国家里，唯有出身和血统才能给予一个白人的种种机遇，都在他面前赫然展现出来。但他的品格真金成色十足，绝不肯为了祖母所能赐予的最丰厚的好处，去背弃自己母亲的种族。

医学大会开幕之初，格雷沙姆医生便结识了拉蒂默医生，为他坦率和蔼的风度所吸引。一天早晨，两人交谈时，格雷沙姆医生得知了他身世中的几个要点；这些非但没有使他心生疏远，反而加深了他对这位年轻医生的钦佩。眼看拉特罗布医生同他相处得那般融洽，格雷沙姆医生暗自觉着有趣；但两人约定，对他的种族渊源保持缄默，直到时机成熟、打算挑明为止；而拉特罗布医生这一记显眼的失言，让他们乐不可支。

## IX Eugene Leroy and Alfred Lorraine / 第九章 尤金·勒罗伊与阿尔弗雷德·洛兰

===Original===
Nearly twenty years before the war, two young men, of French and Spanish descent, sat conversing on a large verandah which surrounded an ancient home on the Mississippi River. It was French in its style of architecture, large and rambling, with no hint of modern improvements.

The owner of the house was the only heir of a Creole planter. He had come into possession of an inheritance consisting of vast baronial estates, bank stock, and a large number of slaves. Eugene Leroy, being deprived of his parents, was left, at an early age, to the care of a distant relative, who had sent him to school and college, and who occasionally invited him to spend his vacations at his home. But Eugene generally declined his invitations, as he preferred spending his vacations at the watering places in the North, with their fashionable and not always innocent gayeties. Young, vivacious, impulsive, and undisciplined, without the restraining influence of a mother’s love or the guidance of a father’s hand, Leroy found himself, when his college days were over, in the dangerous position of a young man with vast possessions, abundant leisure, unsettled principles, and uncontrolled desires. He had no other object than to extract from life its most seductive draughts of ease and pleasure. His companion, who sat opposite him on the verandah, quietly smoking a cigar, was a remote cousin, a few years older than himself, the warmth of whose Southern temperament had been modified by an infusion of Northern blood.

Eugene was careless, liberal, and impatient of details, while his companion and cousin, Alfred Lorraine, was selfish, eager, keen, and alert; also hard, cold, methodical, and ever ready to grasp the main chance. Yet, notwithstanding the difference between them, they had formed a warm friendship for each other.

===Chinese===
战争爆发前将近二十年，两个法国与西班牙血统的青年，坐在密西西比河（Mississippi River）畔一座老宅宽阔的游廊上交谈。宅子是法国式的建筑风格，轩敞而散漫铺展，丝毫不见现代改良的痕迹。

宅子的主人是一位克里奥尔（Creole）种植园主的独嗣。他继承到手的遗产，包括辽阔如领地般的庄园、银行股票和一大批奴隶。尤金·勒罗伊（Eugene Leroy）自幼失怙失恃，小小年纪便被托付给一位远房亲戚照料；亲戚送他上了中学和大学，偶尔也请他到自己家中消磨假期。但尤金往往谢绝他的邀请，他更喜欢到北方各处的避暑胜地去度过假期——那里流行着时髦的、并不总是清白的寻欢作乐。勒罗伊年轻、活泼、冲动而无人管束，既没有母爱的约束，也没有父亲之手的引导；大学岁月一结束，他便发现自己落到了一种危险的境地：一个坐拥巨万家财、闲暇充裕、原则未定、欲望无度的年轻人。他别无他求，只想从人生中榨取出安逸与享乐那最醉人的一杯杯甘醇。坐在游廊对面、静静抽着雪茄的同伴，是他的一位远房表兄，比他年长几岁；那南方人脾性里的热性，因掺入了一支北方血脉而有所冲淡。

尤金疏阔、手松、不耐烦琐细；而他的同伴兼表兄阿尔弗雷德·洛兰（Alfred Lorraine），则自私、急切、精明而机警，同时又冷酷、严峻、一丝不苟，随时准备抓住那最有利的机会。然而，尽管两人性情如此不同，他们之间却结下了一场火热的友谊。

===Original===
“Alfred,” said Eugene, “I am going to be married.”

Lorraine opened his eyes with sudden wonder, and exclaimed: “Well, that’s the latest thing out! Who is the fortunate lady who has bound you with her silken fetters? Is it one of those beautiful Creole girls who were visiting Augustine’s plantation last winter? I watched you during our visit there and thought that you could not be proof against their attractions. Which is your choice? It would puzzle me to judge between the two. They had splendid eyes, dark, luminous, and languishing; lovely complexions and magnificent hair. Both were delightful in their manners, refined and cultured, with an air of vivacity mingled with their repose of manner which was perfectly charming. As the law only allows us one, which is your choice? Miss Annette has more force than her sister, and if I could afford the luxury of a wife she would be my choice.”

“Ah, Alf,” said Eugene, “I see that you are a practical business man. In marrying you want a wife to assist you as an efficient plantation mistress. One who would tolerate no waste in the kitchen and no disorder in the parlor.”

===Chinese===
“阿尔弗雷德，”尤金说，“我要结婚了。”

洛兰惊得睁大了眼睛，嚷道：“嗬，这可是最新鲜的新闻！哪位幸运的姑娘用她丝绒般的枷锁缚住了你？是去年冬天到奥古斯丁（Augustine）家种植园做客的那两位美丽的克里奥尔姑娘之一吗？我们做客那阵子，我一直留意着你，心想她们的魅力你断然招架不住。你选的是哪一位？要我在这一双之间评个高下，还真难住我了。她们都有一双极美的眼睛，幽深、明亮、含情脉脉；肤色可爱，头发浓丽。两人仪态都可人，娴雅而有教养，娴静的仪态里透着一股活泼之气，真是迷人极了。法律只许我们娶一个，你选哪位？安妮特小姐（Miss Annette）比她妹妹更有魄力，倘若我养得起娶妻这份奢侈，我就选她。”

“啊，阿尔夫，”尤金说，“我看你是个务实的生意人。娶妻要娶一个能干的女主人做你的帮手，厨房里容不得半点浪费、客厅里容不得一丝凌乱的那一种。”

===Original===
“Exactly so,” responded Lorraine; “I am too poor to marry a mere parlor ornament. You can afford to do it; I cannot.”

“Nonsense, if I were as poor as a church mouse I would marry the woman I love.”

“Very fine sentiments,” said Lorraine, “and were I as rich as you I would indulge in them also. You know, when my father died I had great expectations. We had always lived in good style, and I never thought for a moment he was not a rich man, but when his estate was settled I found it was greatly involved, and I was forced to face an uncertain future, with scarcely a dollar to call my own. Land, negroes, cattle, and horses all went under the hammer. The only thing I retained was the education I received at the North; that was my father’s best investment, and all my stock in trade. With that only as an outfit, it would be madness for me to think of marrying one of those lovely girls. They remind me of beautiful canary birds, charming and pretty, but not fitted for the wear and tear of plantation life. Well, which is your choice?”

===Chinese===
“正是这样，”洛兰应道，“我太穷，娶不起一件光是摆在客厅里的摆设。你养得起；我养不起。”

“胡说，就算我穷得像教堂里的老鼠，我也要娶我所爱的女人。”

“好漂亮的高论，”洛兰说，“我要是跟你一样阔，我也会来这一套。你知道，我父亲去世时，我还抱着继承一大笔家业的指望。我们家一向过得讲究，我从来一刻也没想过他不是个有钱人；可是等到家产清算下来，我才发现负债累累，我只得去面对一个前途未卜的将来，自己名下几乎连一块钱都没有。田地、黑奴、牛群和马匹，统统上了拍卖台。我保留下来的唯一东西，是在北方受的那份教育；那是我父亲最划算的一笔投资，也是我全部的本钱。随身行装只有这一点，还想去娶那样一位可爱的姑娘，那真是发疯。她们叫我想起美丽的金丝雀，娇媚好看，却经不起种植园生活的操磨。好了，你到底选的是哪一位？”

===Original===
“Neither,” replied Eugene.

“Then, is it that magnificent looking widow from New Orleans, whom we met before you had that terrible spell of sickness and to whom you appeared so devoted?”

“Not at all. I have not heard from her since that summer. She was fascinating and handsome, but fearfully high strung.”

“Were you afraid of her?”

===Chinese===
“哪位都不是，”尤金答道。

“那么，是新奥尔良（New Orleans）那位丰姿夺人的寡妇了？我们遇见她，是在你害那场大病之前；你对她显得一片痴心。”

“根本不是。打那年夏天起，我就再没得到过她的音信。她迷人、漂亮，可是性子烈得吓人。”

“你怕她？”

===Original===
“No; but I valued my happiness too much to trust it in her hands.”

“Sour grapes!” said Lorraine.

“No! but I think that slavery and the lack of outside interests are beginning to tell on the lives of our women. They lean too much on their slaves, have too much irresponsible power in their hands, are narrowed and compressed by the routine of plantation life and the lack of intellectual stimulus.”

“Yes, Eugene, when I see what other women are doing in the fields of literature and art, I cannot help thinking an amount of brain power has been held in check among us. Yet I cannot abide those Northern women, with their suffrage views and abolition cant. They just shock me.”

===Chinese===
“不怕；只是我把自己的幸福看得太重，不敢托到她手里。”

“酸葡萄！”洛兰说。

“不是！只是我觉得，奴隶制和外界天地与兴味的匮乏，已经开始在我们的女人们身上显出恶果。她们过分倚赖奴隶，手里握着太多不受约束的权力，又被种植园生活的刻板日程和心智激发的缺失，弄得眼界狭窄、心胸压抑。”

“是啊，尤金，每当我看到别的女子在文学和艺术的园地里大展身手，我就不禁想，我们这里有多少聪明才智被压住了，施展不开。可我又受不了那些北方女人，满嘴妇女参政的见解和废奴派的滥调。她们简直叫我恶心。”

===Original===
“But your mother was a Northern woman,” said Eugene.

“Yes; but she got bravely over her Northern ideas. As I remember her, she was just as much a Southerner as if she had been to the manor born. She came here as a schoolteacher, but soon after she came she married my father. He was easy and indulgent with his servants, and held them with a very loose rein. But my mother was firm and energetic. She made the niggers move around. No shirking nor dawdling with her. When my father died, she took matters in hand, but she only outlived him a few months. If she had lived I believe that she would have retrieved our fortune. I know that she had more executive ability than my father. He was very squeamish about selling his servants, but she would have put every one of them in her pocket before permitting them to eat her out of house and home. But whom *are* you going to marry?”

“A young lady who graduates from a Northern seminary next week,” responded Eugene.

===Chinese===
“可你母亲就是北方人啊，”尤金说。

“是；可她把她那些北方观念利利索索地抛掉了。据我记得的，她活脱脱就是个南方人，仿佛生在庄园、长在庄园似的。她来的时候是做女教师的，可来了不久就嫁给了我父亲。父亲待仆役宽和纵容，缰绳牵得极松。我母亲却坚定而有魄力。她叫那些黑鬼一个个都得动起来。在她跟前，谁也别想偷懒磨蹭。父亲死后，她把家事一把抓了起来，可惜她只比他多活了几个月。她若是活着，我相信准能把我们的家业重新振兴起来。我知道，论办事的才干，她比我父亲强。父亲卖起自己的仆役来总是顾虑重重，她却准会抢在那些人把家底吃光之前，把他们一个个都装进自己口袋里变卖成钱。可你究竟要娶的是谁？”

“一位下星期就要从北方一所女子学校毕业的年轻小姐，”尤金答道。

===Original===
“I think you are very selfish,” said Lorraine. “You might have invited a fellow to go with you to be your best man.”

“The wedding is to be strictly private. The lady whom I am to marry has negro blood in her veins.”

“The devil she has!” exclaimed Lorraine, starting to his feet, and looking incredulously on the face of Leroy. “Are you in earnest? Surely you must be jesting.”

===Chinese===
“我看你太自私了，”洛兰说。“怎么不请个伙伴跟你一道去，给你做伴郎。”

“婚礼要办得绝对私密。我要娶的这位小姐，血管里流着黑人的血。”

“见鬼，她有！”洛兰霍地站起身来，满脸不敢相信地盯着勒罗伊的脸，嚷道：“你是当真的？你准是在说笑。”

===Original===
“I am certainly in earnest,” answered Eugene Leroy. “I mean every word I say.”

“Oh, it can’t be possible! Are you mad?” exclaimed Lorraine.

“Never was saner in my life.”

“What under heaven could have possessed you to do such a foolish thing? Where did she come from.”

===Chinese===
“我当然是当真的，”尤金·勒罗伊答道。“我说的每一个字都算数。”

“哦，这不可能！你疯了吗？”洛兰嚷道。

“我这辈子从没这么清醒过。”

“天底下到底是什么迷了你的心窍，叫你干出这种蠢事？她又是打哪儿来的。”

===Original===
“Right here, on this plantation. But I have educated and manumitted her, and I intend marrying her.”

“Why, Eugene, it is impossible that you can have an idea of marrying one of your slaves. Why, man, she is your property, to have and to hold to all intents and purposes. Are you not satisfied with the power and possession the law gives you?”

“No. Although the law makes her helpless in my hands, to me her defenselessness is her best defense.”

===Chinese===
“就在这儿，就在这座种植园上。不过我已经让她受了教育、解除了她的奴籍，我打算娶她。”

“嗐，尤金，你竟然会起意娶自己的一个奴隶，这不可能。嗐，老兄，她是你的财产，不折不扣，任凭你占有、任凭你保有。法律给你的权柄和占有，你还不满足吗？”

“不满足。法律虽使她在我手里全然无力自卫，可在我看来，她的无力自卫，正是她最好的防卫。”

===Original===
“Eugene, we have known each other all of our lives, and, although I have always regarded you as eccentric, I never saw you so completely off your balance before. The idea of you, with your proud family name, your vast wealth in land and negroes, intending to marry one of them, is a mystery I cannot solve. Do explain to me why you are going to take this extremely strange and foolish step.”

“You never saw Marie?”

“No; and I don’t want to.”

“She is very beautiful. In the North no one would suspect that she has one drop of negro blood in her veins, but here, where I am known, to marry her is to lose caste. I could live with her, and not incur much if any social opprobrium. Society would wink at the transgression, even if after she had become the mother of my children I should cast her off and send her and them to the auction block.”

===Chinese===
“尤金，我们俩打小相识，这些年来我虽一向当你是个怪僻的人，却从没见你这样完全失了常态。想想你——顶着这样高贵的家姓，坐拥这样的田产黑奴巨富——竟然要娶他们中间的一个，这是我一桩解不开的谜。千万给我讲讲，你为什么要迈出这极端荒唐、极端愚蠢的一步。”

“你没见过玛丽（Marie）？”

“没有；也不想见。”

“她非常美。在北方，没有人会疑心她血管里掺着一滴黑人的血；可是在这里，在认识我的地方，娶她就是自跌身份。我可以和她同居，几乎不会招来什么社会的责难。哪怕在她为我生儿育女之后，我把她休弃、把她和孩子们一并送上拍卖台，社会对这个罪过也只会睁一只眼闭一只眼。”

===Original===
“Men,” replied Lorraine, “would merely shrug their shoulders; women would say you had been sowing your wild oats. Your money, like charity, would cover a multitude of faults.”

“But if I make her my lawful wife and recognize her children as my legitimate heirs, I subject myself to social ostracism and a senseless persecution. We Americans boast of freedom, and yet here is a woman whom I love as I never loved any other human being, but both law and public opinion debar me from following the inclination of my heart. She is beautiful, faithful, and pure, and yet all that society will tolerate is what I would scorn to do.”

“But has not society the right to guard the purity of its blood by the rigid exclusion of an alien race?”

===Chinese===
“男人们，”洛兰答道，“只会耸耸肩膀；女人们会说，你不过是撒了撒野燕麦，年轻时荒唐了一阵。你的钱财就像爱心，足以遮掩许许多多的过错。”

“可是，如果我让她做我的合法妻子、认她的孩子做我的正当继承人，我就要把自个儿置于社会放逐和无理迫害之下。我们美国人夸口自由，然而眼前就是这样一个女人——我爱她胜过爱世上任何一个人——法律和舆论却双双拦住我，不许我随自己心之所向。她美丽、忠贞而纯洁，而社会所能容忍的一切，恰恰是我所耻于去做的。”

“可是，社会为了保全血统的纯净，把异族严厉排斥在外——难道它就没有这个权利吗？”

===Original===
“Excluding it! How?” asked Eugene.

“By debarring it from social intercourse.”

“Perhaps it has,” continued Eugene, “but should not society have a greater ban for those who, by consorting with an alien race, rob their offspring of a right to their names and to an inheritance in their property, and who fix their social status among an enslaved and outcast race? Don’t eye me so curiously; I am not losing my senses.”

“I think you have done that already,” said Lorraine. “Don’t you know that if she is as fair as a lily, beautiful as a houri, and chaste as ice, that still she is a negro?”

===Chinese===
“排斥！”尤金问。“怎么个排斥法？”

“不许它进入社交往来。”

“也许它有这个权利，”尤金接着说，“可是，有些人同异族结合，剥夺了亲生子女承袭他们姓氏、继承他们产业的权利，把子女的社会地位钉死在一个受奴役、被遗弃的种族中间——对这样的人，社会难道不该下一道更重的禁令吗？别这样好奇地盯着我瞧；我并没有神志不清。”

“我看你早就神志不清了，”洛兰说。“难道你不知道，哪怕她白得像百合，美得像天园的仙女，贞洁得像冰，她仍然是个黑人？”

===Original===
“Oh, come now; she isn’t much of a negro.”

“It doesn’t matter, however. One drop of negro blood in her veins curses all the rest.”

“I know it,” said Eugene, sadly, “but I have weighed the consequences, and am prepared to take them.”

“Well, Eugene, your course is *so* singular! I do wish that you would tell me why you take this unprecedented step?”

===Chinese===
“嗳，得了；她也没有多少黑人的成分。”

“可那不要紧。她血管里只要有一滴黑人的血，其余的血便全都遭了咒。”

“我知道，”尤金凄然说，“可后果我都掂量过了，也准备一力承担。”

“唉，尤金，你这步棋走得实在离奇！我真心盼着你告诉我，你为什么要迈出这前所未有的一步？”

===Original===
Eugene laid aside his cigar, looked thoughtfully at Lorraine, and said, “Well, Alfred, as we are kinsmen and lifelong friends, I will not resent your asking my reason for doing that which seems to you the climax of absurdity, and if you will have the patience to listen I will tell you.”

“Proceed, I am all attention.”

“My father died,” said Eugene, “as you know, when I was too young to know his loss or feel his care and, being an only child, I was petted and spoiled. I grew up to be wayward, self-indulgent, proud, and imperious. I went from home and made many friends both at college and in foreign lands. I was well supplied with money and, never having been forced to earn it, was ignorant of its value and careless of its use. My lavish expenditures and liberal benefactions attracted to me a number of parasites, and men older than myself led me into the paths of vice, and taught me how to gather the flowers of sin which blossom around the borders of hell. In a word, I left my home unwarned and unarmed against the seductions of vice. I returned an initiated devotee to debasing pleasures. Years of my life were passed in foreign lands; years in which my soul slumbered and seemed pervaded with a moral paralysis; years, the memory of which fills my soul with sorrow and shame. I went to the capitals of the old world to see life, but in seeing life I became acquainted with death, the death of true manliness and self-respect. You look astonished; but I tell you, Alf, there is many a poor clodhopper, on whom are the dust and grime of unremitting toil, who feels more self-respect and true manliness than many of us with our family prestige, social position, and proud ancestral halls. After I had lived abroad for years, I returned a broken-down young man, prematurely old, my constitution a perfect wreck. A life of folly and dissipation was telling fearfully upon me. My friends shrank from me in dismay. I was sick nigh unto death, and had it not been for Marie’s care I am certain that I should have died. She followed me down to the borders of the grave, and won me back to life and health. I was slow in recovering and, during the time, I had ample space for reflection, and the past unrolled itself before me. I resolved, over the wreck and ruin of my past life, to build a better and brighter future. Marie had a voice of remarkable sweetness, although it lacked culture. Often when I was nervous and restless I would have her sing some of those weird and plaintive melodies which she had learned from the plantation negroes. Sometimes I encouraged her to talk, and I was surprised at the native vigor of her intellect. By degrees I became acquainted with her history. She was all alone in the world. She had no recollection of her father, but remembered being torn from her mother while clinging to her dress. The trader who bought her mother did not wish to buy her. She remembered having a brother, with whom she used to play, but she had been separated from him also, and since then had lost all trace of them. After she was sold from her mother she became the property of an excellent old lady, who seems to have been very careful to imbue her mind with good principles; a woman who loved purity, not only for her own daughters, but also for the defenseless girls in her home. I believe it was the lady’s intention to have freed Marie at her death, but she died suddenly, and, the estate being involved, she was sold with it and fell into the hands of my agent. I became deeply interested in her when I heard her story, and began to pity her.”

===Chinese===
尤金搁下雪茄，若有所思地望着洛兰，说道：“好吧，阿尔弗雷德，念在你我是亲眷，又是终身的朋友，你问我为什么做这件在你眼里荒唐透顶的事，我不见怪。你若有耐心听，我就讲给你听。”

“讲吧，我洗耳恭听。”

“我父亲去世时，”尤金说，“你知道，我年纪还小，既不懂得失怙之痛，也未曾感受过他的照拂；又是独生子，从小被人宠爱娇惯。我长成了一个任性、放纵、高傲而专横的人。我离家在外，在大学里、在异国结交了许多朋友。我手头钱向来宽裕，又从不必自己去挣，不知道它的价值，用起来也毫不在意。我出手阔绰、馈赠慷慨，招来了身边一班食客；比我年长的人们引我走上了邪路，教我采撷那些在地狱边缘盛开的罪恶之花。一句话，我离开家门时，对于罪恶的诱惑既无人警告，也全无防备；我归来时，已成了堕落欢娱中入了门的行家。我的生命有多年在异国度过——那些年，我的灵魂沉睡着，仿佛浸透了一种道德上的麻痹；那些年，一回忆起来，便使我的灵魂充满悲哀与羞耻。我到旧世界的都城去见世面，可在见识人生的同时，我认识了死亡——真正的男子气概与自尊的死亡。你一脸惊讶；可我告诉你，阿尔夫，有多少穷苦的庄稼汉，身上带着终日劳作不歇的尘土和汗垢，他们心里的自尊和真正的男子气概，却胜过我们许多拥有家声、地位和气派祖宅的人。我在国外住了多年之后回来，已经是个垮掉的年轻人，未老先衰，体格彻底毁了。荒唐和纵欲的生活在我身上显出可怕的结果。朋友们惊慌地躲开我。我病得几乎死去，若不是玛丽的照料，我肯定活不成了。她跟着我一路走到坟墓的边上，又把我赢回了生命和健康。我康复得很慢，在那段日子里，我有了充分的余暇反省，往事在我眼前一一铺展开来。我决意在我过去生活的废墟和残骸之上，建立起一个更美好、更光明的未来。玛丽有一副异常甜美的嗓音，虽然缺少调教。我烦躁不安的时候，常常让她唱那些奇异而哀婉的曲调，那是她从种植园的黑人们那里学来的。有时我引她说话，她天资的健旺令我吃惊。我渐渐知道了她的身世。她在世上孑然一身。她对父亲毫无记忆，只记得自己紧紧揪着母亲的衣裙、被人从母亲身边撕扯走的那一幕。买下她母亲的贩子本不想连她一起买。她记得自己有个弟弟，姐弟俩从前常在一起玩，可后来她也和他分开了，从此再没有得到他们的半点踪迹。她被从母亲身边卖走之后，成了一位极好的老夫人的财产；那位夫人看来极用心地以良善的原则培植她的心灵，是一位珍重贞洁的女子——不仅为自己的女儿们珍重，也为她家中那些无力自卫的姑娘们珍重。我相信老夫人的本意，是要在临终时还玛丽自由；可她死得突然，家产又负了债，玛丽便随着产业一并卖掉，落到了我代理人的手里。听了她的身世，我对她深深关切起来，开始怜惜她。”

===Original===
“And I suppose love sprang from pity.”

“I not only pitied her, but I learned to respect her. I had met with beautiful women in the halls of wealth and fashion, both at home and abroad, but there was something in her different from all my experience of womanhood.”

“I should think so,” said Lorraine, with a sneer; “but I should like to know what it was.”

“It was something such as I have seen in old cathedrals, lighting up the beauty of a saintly face. A light which the poet tells was never seen on land or sea. I thought of this beautiful and defenseless girl adrift in the power of a reckless man, who, with all the advantages of wealth and education, had trailed his manhood in the dust, and she, with simple, childlike faith in the Unseen, seemed to be so good and pure that she commanded my respect and won my heart. In her presence every base and unholy passion died, subdued by the supremacy of her virtue.”

===Chinese===
“我猜，爱情就是从怜悯里生出来的。”

“我不但怜惜她，还渐渐敬重她。国内外富贵时髦的厅堂里，美丽的女子我见过许多，可她身上有一种东西，与我历来所识的一切女子都不同。”

“我想也是，”洛兰讥诮地说；“不过我倒想知道，那是什么。”

“那是一种我在古老的大教堂里见过的东西，把一张圣徒面容的美照亮了。那是诗人笔下从未照临过陆地或海洋的光辉。我想到这个美丽而无依的姑娘，飘零在一个肆无忌惮的男人权势之中——那人坐拥财富与教育的全部优势，却把自己的男子气概拖进了尘泥——而她，对那位肉眼看不见的主怀着单纯的、孩子般的信心，那样善良、那样纯洁，令我不能不敬重她，赢得了我的心。在她面前，一切卑污不洁的欲念都归于寂灭，被她德性的至高力量所降伏。”

===Original===
“Why, Eugene, what has come over you? Talking of the virtue of these quadroon girls! You have lived so long in the North and abroad, that you seem to have lost the cue of our Southern life. Don’t you know that these beautiful girls have been the curse of our homes? You have no idea of the hearts which are wrung by their presence.”

“But, Alfred, suppose it is so. Are they to blame for it? What can any woman do when she is placed in the hands of an irresponsible master; when she knows that resistance is vain? Yes, Alfred, I agree with you, these women are the bane of our Southern civilization; but they are the victims and we are the criminals.”

“I think from the airs that some of them put on when they get a chance, that they are very willing victims.”

“So much the worse for our institution. If it is cruel to debase a hapless victim, it is an increase of cruelty to make her contented with her degradation. Let me tell you, Alf, you cannot wrong or degrade a woman without wronging or degrading yourself.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，尤金，你这是怎么了？居然谈论起这些夸德隆姑娘（quadroon girls，有黑人血统四分之一的混血姑娘）的贞洁来！你在北方和外国住得太久，南方生活的门道你好像都忘了。你难道不知道，这些美丽的姑娘一向是我们家庭的祸根？你想象不到，她们的存在绞碎了多少人的心。”

“可是，阿尔弗雷德，就算是这样吧。这难道能怪她们吗？一个女人落在一个全无约束的主人手里，明知反抗也是徒劳，她还能怎么样？是的，阿尔弗雷德，我同意你的话，这些女子是我们南方文明的祸害；可她们是牺牲者，我们才是罪人。”

“照我看，她们里头有些人一得了机会就摆出的那副神气，倒像是心甘情愿的牺牲者哩。”

“那我们的这个制度就更加不堪了。作践一个不幸的牺牲者已是残忍，让她对自己的屈辱心满意足，更是残忍上加残忍。我告诉你，阿尔夫，你委屈也好、作践也好，伤害一个妇人，就不可能不同时委屈、作践你自己。”

===Original===
“What is the matter with you, Eugene? Are you thinking of taking priest’s orders?”

“No, Alf,” said Eugene, rising and rapidly pacing the floor, “you may defend the system as much as you please, but you cannot deny that the circumstances it creates, and the temptations it affords, are sapping our strength and undermining our character.”

“That may be true,” said Lorraine, somewhat irritably, “but you had better be careful how you air your Northern notions in public.”

“Why so?”

===Chinese===
“你到底是怎么了，尤金？莫非是想领受神父的圣职？”

“不是，阿尔夫，”尤金说着站起身来，疾步在地板上来回走着，“你尽可以随意替这个制度辩护，可你无法否认：它造出的种种境况、它提供的种种诱惑，正在掏空我们的元气、蛀蚀我们的品格。”

“也许是真，”洛兰有些恼火地说，“不过你最好小心些，别到公开场合去宣扬你那些北方观念。”

“为什么？”

===Original===
“Because public opinion is too sensitive to tolerate any such discussions.”

“And is not that a proof that we are at fault with respect to our institutions?”

“I don’t know. I only know we are living in the midst of a magazine of powder, and it is not safe to enter it with a lighted candle.”

“Let me proceed with my story,” continued Eugene. “During the long months in which I was convalescing, I was left almost entirely to the companionship of Marie. In my library I found a Bible, which I began to read from curiosity, but my curiosity deepened into interest when I saw the rapt expression on Marie’s face. I saw in it a loving response to sentiments to which I was a stranger. In the meantime my conscience was awakened, and I scorned to take advantage of her defenselessness. I felt that I owed my life to her faithful care, and I resolved to take her North, manumit, educate, and marry her. I sent her to a Northern academy, but as soon as some of the pupils found that she was colored, objections were raised, and the principal was compelled to dismiss her. During my search for a school I heard of one where three girls of mixed blood were pursuing their studies, every one of whom would have been ignominiously dismissed had their connection with the negro race been known. But I determined to run no risks. I found a school where her connection with the negro race would be no bar to her advancement. She graduates next week, and I intend to marry her before I return home. She was faithful when others were faithless, stood by me when others deserted me to die in loneliness and neglect, and now I am about to reward her care with all the love and devotion it is in my power to bestow. That is why I am about to marry my faithful and devoted nurse, who snatched me from the jaws of death. Now that I have told you my story, what say you?”

===Chinese===
“因为舆论太敏感，容不得这类议论。”

“那不正是一个凭据，说明我们的制度出了毛病吗？”

“我不知道。我只知道，我们正住在一座火药库当中，手里举着点亮的蜡烛进去，可不太平。”

“让我把我的故事讲下去，”尤金接着说。“在我养病的那漫长的几个月里，陪伴我的几乎只有玛丽。我在书房里发现了一部《圣经》，起初出于好奇翻开来读；可当我看见玛丽脸上那出神的表情时，好奇便深化成了兴味。我在那神情里看见一种满怀爱意的回应，回应着那些我素不相识的情感。与此同时，我的良知觉醒了，我耻于趁她无力自卫而占她的便宜。我觉得我的性命是她忠心的看护救回来的，便决意带她到北方去，解除她的奴籍，让她受教育，然后娶她。我把她送进了北方的一所学校；可学生中间刚有人发现她是有色人种，反对之声便起来了，校长被迫让她退了学。我在另觅学校的那阵子，听说有一所学校里有三个混血姑娘在读着书，她们与黑人的血缘若被知晓，个个都会蒙羞被逐。可我打定主意，不再冒任何风险。我找到了一所学校，在那里，她与黑人的血缘不会妨碍她的上进。她下星期毕业，我打算在回家之前娶她。别人离心的时候，她忠心不改；别人撇下我、任我孤零零无人照管地死去的时候，她守在我身边。如今，我要以我所能给予的全部爱情与忠诚，报答她的看护。这就是我为什么要娶我这位忠心耿耿的看护——她把我从死神口中夺了回来。现在我把我的故事都告诉了你，你还有什么话说？”

===Original===
“Madness and folly inconceivable!” exclaimed Lorraine.

“What to you is madness and folly is perfect sanity with me. After all, Alf, is there not an amount of unreason in our prejudices?”

“That may be true; but I wasn’t reasoned into it, and I do not expect to be reasoned out of it.”

“Will you accompany me North?”

===Chinese===
“不可理喻的疯狂和愚蠢！”洛兰嚷道。

“在你看来是疯狂和愚蠢，在我看来却是十足的清醒。说到底，阿尔夫，我们的偏见里，难道就没有几分不讲道理吗？”

“也许是真；可我当初并不是让人讲道理讲成这样的，我也不指望能让人讲道理讲回来。”

“你陪我到北方去吗？”

===Original===
“No; except to put you in an insane asylum. You are the greatest crank out,” said Lorraine, thoroughly disgusted.

“No, thank you; I’m all right. I expect to start North tomorrow. You had better come and go.”

“I would rather follow you to your grave,” replied Lorraine, hotly, while an expression of ineffable scorn passed over his cold, proud face.

===Chinese===
“不去；除非是把你们送进疯人院去。你真是天字第一号的大怪人，”洛兰说，厌恶透了。

“不用了，谢谢；我好得很。我打算明天动身北上。你还是跟我走一趟的好。”

“我宁可跟着你进坟墓，”洛兰恼怒地答道；一阵说不尽的鄙夷掠过他那张冷淡而高傲的脸。

## XVII Flames in the Schoolroom / 第十七章 教室里的火焰

===Original===
“Good morning,” said Dr. Gresham, approaching Robert and Iola. “How are you both? You have mended rapidly,” turning to Robert, “but then it was only a flesh wound. Your general health being good, and your blood in excellent condition, it was not hard for you to rally.”

“Where have you been, Doctor? I have a faint recollection of having seen you on the morning I was brought in from the field, but not since.”

“I have been on a furlough. I was running down through exhaustion and overwork, and I was compelled to go home for a few weeks’ rest. But now, as they are about to close the hospital, I shall be permanently relieved. I am glad that this cruel strife is over. It seemed as if I had lived through ages during these last few years. In the early part of the war I lost my arm by a stray shot, and my armless sleeve is one of the mementos of battle I shall carry with me through life. Miss Leroy,” he continued, turning respectfully to Iola, “would you permit me to ask you, as I would have someone ask my sister under the same circumstances, if you have matured any plans for the future, or if I can be of the least service to you? If so, I would be pleased to render you any service in my power.”

“My purpose,” replied Iola, “is to hunt for my mother, and to find her if she is alive. I am willing to go anywhere and do anything to find her. But I will need a standpoint from whence I can send out lines of inquiry. It must take time, in the disordered state of affairs, even to get a clue by which I may discover her whereabouts.”

===Chinese===
“早上好，”格雷沙姆医生走近罗伯特和伊奥拉，说道。“你们两位可都好？您康复得很快，”他转向罗伯特，接着说，“不过您受的原是皮肉伤。您身子底子好，气血又旺，将养起来自然不难。”

“医生，您这些日子去哪儿了？我只依稀记得，自己被人从战场上抬回来的那天早晨见过您一面，打那以后就再没见着了。”

“我休了一次假。操劳过度，把身体熬垮了，我只得回家静养几个星期。如今医院行将关闭，我也算永远卸下了这副担子。我很高兴，这场残酷的战事总算结束了。这几年下来，我仿佛活过了好几世。战争初期，一颗流弹夺走了我的一条胳膊；我这只空袖筒，就是我此生要随身携带的战场纪念物之一。勒罗伊小姐，”他继续说道，恭恭敬敬地转向伊奥拉，“倘若我自己的妹妹处在你这样的境地，我也会巴不得有人这样问问她——请容我冒昧问一句：你对将来可曾有什么成熟的打算？可有什么我能效力的地方，哪怕帮上一星半点？若有，凡是我力所能及的事，我都乐意为你去办。”

“我的打算，”伊奥拉答道，“就是去寻找我的母亲；只要她还活着，就要把她找到。为了找到她，多远的地方我都肯去，什么样的事我都肯做。可我总得有个立足点，好从那里发出一封封寻访的信函。眼下时局这般纷乱，哪怕只求得一条线索、好查明她的下落，也非得费些时日不可。”

===Original===
“How would you like to teach?” asked the Doctor. “Schools are being opened all around us. Numbers of excellent and superior women are coming from the North to engage as teachers of the freed people. Would you be willing to take a school among these people? I think it will be uphill work. I believe it will take generations to get over the duncery of slavery. Some of these poor fellows who came into our camp did not know their right hands from their left, nor their ages, nor even the days of the month. It took me some time, in a number of cases, to understand their language. It saddened my heart to see such ignorance. One day I asked one a question, and he answered, ‘I no shum.’ ”

“What did he mean?” asked Iola.

“That he did not see it,” replied the doctor. “Of course, this does not apply to all of them. Some of them are wide-awake and sharp as steel traps. I think some of that class may be used in helping others.”

“I should be very glad to have an opportunity to teach,” said Iola. “I used to be a great favorite among the colored children on my father’s plantation.”

===Chinese===
“你觉得教书怎么样？”医生问道。“我们四下里到处都在开办学校。一批批品行出众的优秀女子正从北方赶来，为的是给获得自由的人们当教师。你可愿意到这些人中间执掌一所学校？依我看，这是件费力的爬坡活儿。我相信，要消除奴隶制积下的愚昧，非得几代人的工夫不可。有些到我们营里来的可怜人，左右手都分不清，说不出自己的岁数，甚至连一个月里的日子也认不得。有不少回，我费了好些时间才听懂他们的话。见到这般无知，我心里实在难过。有一天，我问其中一个人一个问题，他回答说：‘俺瞅不见。’”

“他这话是什么意思？”伊奥拉问。

“意思是他看不见那东西，”医生答道。“当然，这话并不是说他们人人如此。他们中间有些人却机灵警醒，脑子快得像口利落的钢夹子。我想，那一类人兴许可以派上用场，去帮带帮带旁人。”

“能有机会教书，我真是求之不得，”伊奥拉说。“从前在父亲的种植园里，我向来是最受有色人种孩子们宠爱的人。”

===Original===
In a few days after this conversation the hospital was closed. The sick and convalescent were removed, and Iola obtained a position as a teacher. Very soon Iola realized that while she was heartily appreciated by the freedmen, she was an object of suspicion and dislike to their former owners. The North had conquered by the supremacy of the sword, and the South had bowed to the inevitable. But here was a new army that had come with an invasion of ideas, that had come to supplant ignorance with knowledge, and it was natural that its members should be unwelcome to those who had made it a crime to teach their slaves to read the name of the ever blessed Christ. But Iola had found her work, and the freed men their friend.

When Iola opened her school she took pains to get acquainted with the parents of the children, and she gained their confidence and cooperation. Her face was a passport to their hearts. Ignorant of books, human faces were the scrolls from which they had been reading for ages. They had been the sunshine and shadow of their lives.

Iola had found a schoolroom in the basement of a colored church, where the doors were willingly opened to her. Her pupils came from miles around, ready and anxious to get some “book larnin’.” Some of the old folks were eager to learn, and it was touching to see the eyes which had grown dim under the shadows of slavery, donning spectacles and trying to make out the words. As Iola had nearly all of her life been accustomed to colored children she had no physical repulsions to overcome, no prejudices to conquer in dealing with parents and children. In their simple childish fashion they would bring her fruits and flowers, and gladden her lonely heart with little tokens of affection.

===Chinese===
这番谈话过后没几天，医院便关闭了。病号和康复中的人们被转送他处，伊奥拉谋得了一个教师的职位。没过多久伊奥拉便觉察到：获得自由的人们对她由衷爱戴，可在他们昔日的主人眼里，她却是个招疑忌、惹厌恶的对象。北方凭刀剑的威势完成了征服，南方也向这无可避免的结局低下了头。然而，此刻又开来了一支新的军队——一支挟着思想的入侵而来的军队，要用知识取代愚昧；这支军队的成员，落到那些人跟前自然不受欢迎——要知道，正是那些人，从前把教自家奴隶念出那永蒙福佑的基督圣名，都定成了罪。但伊奥拉找到了她的事业，获得自由的人们也找到了他们的朋友。

伊奥拉开办学校时，特意用心结识孩子们的父母，赢得了他们的信任和配合。她的面孔，就是通向他们心灵的一纸通行证。这些人虽然不识书本，人的面孔却是他们世世代代捧读的经卷——一张张面孔，原是他们生活中的阳光与阴影。

伊奥拉在一座有色人种教堂的地下室里寻得一间教室，教堂的大门甘愿为她敞开。她的学生从方圆几英里外赶来，个个迫不及待，只想学点“书本子学问”。有些老人家也求学心切；看着那双双在奴隶制阴影下熬得昏花的老眼戴上眼镜、费力辨认字句的模样，实在叫人心酸感动。伊奥拉几乎终其一生都习惯了同有色人种的孩子相处，因此同家长和孩子打交道，她既没有生理上的嫌恶要克服，也没有先入为主的偏见要打消。孩子们用他们质朴稚气的方式，给她捎来水果和鲜花，用一件件小小的心意，让她孤寂的心得到欢悦。

===Original===
One day a gentleman came to the school and wished to address the children. Iola suspended the regular order of the school, and the gentleman essayed to talk to them on the achievements of the white race, such as building steamboats and carrying on business. Finally, he asked how they did it?

“They’ve got money,” chorused the children.

“But how did they get it?”

“They took it from us,” chimed the youngsters. Iola smiled, and the gentleman was nonplussed; but he could not deny that one of the powers of knowledge is the power of the strong to oppress the weak.

===Chinese===
有一天，一位先生来到学校，想对孩子们讲几句话。伊奥拉暂停了学校惯常的课序，这位先生便试着对他们讲起白种人的种种成就，比如造汽船、做生意。末了，他问，他们是怎样做到的呢？

“他们有钱，”孩子们齐声喊道。

“可那钱是怎么来的呢？”

“是从我们手里拿去的，”孩子们接口道。伊奥拉微微一笑，那位先生一时语塞；可他无从否认：知识所赋予的力量之一，正是强者用来欺压弱者的力量。

===Original===
The school was soon overcrowded with applicants, and Iola was forced to refuse numbers, because their quarters were too cramped. The school was beginning to lift up the home, for Iola was not satisfied to teach her children only the rudiments of knowledge. She had tried to lay the foundation of good character. But the elements of evil burst upon her loved and cherished work. One night the heavens were lighted with lurid flames, and Iola beheld the school, the pride and joy of her pupils and their parents, a smouldering ruin. Iola gazed with sorrowful dismay on what seemed the cruel work of an incendiary’s torch. While she sat, mournfully contemplating the work of destruction, her children formed a procession, and, passing by the wreck of their school, sang:﻿—

> “Oh, do not be discouraged,
 

 For Jesus is your friend.”

As they sang, the tears sprang to Iola’s eyes, and she said to herself, “I am not despondent of the future of my people; there is too much elasticity in their spirits, too much hope in their hearts, to be crushed out by unreasoning malice.”

===Chinese===
学校很快就让报名的人挤得满满当当；因为场地太窄，伊奥拉不得不回绝掉一批。学校渐渐开始把一个个家庭也托举起来——伊奥拉并不满足于只教孩子们知识的入门功课，她还努力为良好的品格奠下根基。然而，恶的种种势力，向她所热爱、所珍视的事业骤然袭来。一天夜里，天空被不祥的烈焰照得通红，伊奥拉眼睁睁看着那所学校——她的学生和学生家长的骄傲与欢乐——化作一片闷烧的废墟。伊奥拉悲恸而惊愕地凝视着那似乎是纵火者火把干下的残酷勾当。她坐在那里，凄然望着这场劫毁；这时，她的学生们排成一列队伍，走过他们学校的残骸，唱道：

> “哦，切莫灰心丧气，
 

 因为耶稣是你们的朋友。”

听着歌声，泪水涌上了伊奥拉的眼眶。她对自己说：“我并不为我的人民的前途灰心；他们的精神里有的是韧劲，他们的心里有的是希望，任凭什么蛮不讲理的歹毒，也压不垮、碾不灭。”

## XXX Friends in Council / 第三十章 朋友的议事会

===Original===
On the following Friday evening, Mr. Stillman’s pleasant, spacious parlors were filled to overflowing with a select company of earnest men and women deeply interested in the welfare of the race.
Bishop Tunster had prepared a paper on “Negro Emigration.” Dr. Latimer opened the discussion by speaking favorably of some of the salient points, but said:﻿—
“I do not believe self-exilement is the true remedy for the wrongs of the negro. Where should he go if he left this country?”
“Go to Africa,” replied Bishop Tunster, in his bluff, hearty tones. “I believe that Africa is to be redeemed to civilization, and that the negro is to be gathered into the family of nations and recognized as a man and a brother.”

===Chinese===
接下来的那个星期五晚上，斯蒂尔曼先生（Mr. Stillman）那几间敞亮宜人的客厅里挤得满满当当，来的是一班经过挑选的宾客——恳挚热诚的男男女女，无不深切关怀着这个种族的福祉。

滕斯特主教（Bishop Tunster）预备了一篇论文，题为《黑人的外迁》。拉蒂默医生率先发言，对其中几个要点表示赞许，随后说道：——

“我不认为自我流放是医治黑人冤屈的真正良方。倘若他离开这个国家，又能到哪里去呢？”

“到非洲去，”滕斯特主教用他那直率热诚的口吻答道，“我相信非洲终将蒙救赎而归入文明，黑人终将被接纳进万国之林，被承认是人、是兄弟。”

===Original===
“Go to Africa?” repeated Professor Langhorne, of Georgia. “Does the United States own one foot of African soil? And have we not been investing our blood in the country for ages?”
“I am in favor of missionary efforts,” said Professor Gradnor, of North Carolina, “for the redemption of Africa, but I see no reason for expatriating ourselves because some persons do not admire the color of our skins.”
“I do not believe,” said Mr. Stillman, “in emptying on the shores of Africa a horde of ignorant, poverty-stricken people, as missionaries of civilization or Christianity. And while I am in favor of missionary efforts, there is need here for the best heart and brain to work in unison for justice and righteousness.”
“America,” said Miss Delany, “is the best field for human development. God has not heaped up our mountains with such grandeur, flooded our rivers with such majesty, crowned our valleys with such fertility, enriched our mines with such wealth, that they should only minister to grasping greed and sensuous enjoyment.”

===Chinese===
“到非洲去？”佐治亚（Georgia）的朗霍恩教授（Professor Langhorne）反问道。“美利坚合众国在非洲可有一寸国土？我们世世代代往这个国家里倾注自己的血，难道倾注得还不够吗？”

“我赞成开展传教事业，”北卡罗来纳的格拉德诺教授（Professor Gradnor）说，“去救赎非洲；可若因为有些人不喜欢我们的肤色，我们就要把自己放逐出境，我看不出有什么理由。”

“我不赞成，”斯蒂尔曼先生说，“把一大群无知无识、一贫如洗的人倾倒在非洲海岸上，去充任文明或基督教的传教士。我虽然赞成传教事业，可这里同样需要最优秀的心灵与头脑，齐心协力地伸张正义与公道。”

“美利坚，”德拉尼小姐说，“是人类发展的最佳天地。上帝把我们的群山堆垒得如此雄伟，让我们的江河漫溢得如此壮丽，为我们的谷地加冕上如此的丰饶，使我们的矿藏蓄积了如此的财富，绝不是要这一切单单去侍奉巧取豪夺的贪欲与声色口腹的享乐。”


===Original===
“Climate, soil, and physical environments,” said Professor Gradnor, “have much to do with shaping national characteristics. If in Africa, under a tropical sun, the negro has lagged behind other races in the march of civilization, at least for once in his history he has, in this country, the privilege of using climatic advantages and developing under new conditions.”
“Yes,” replied Dr. Latimer, “and I do not wish our people to become restless and unsettled before they have tried one generation of freedom.”
“I am always glad,” said Mr. Forest, a tall, distinguished-looking gentleman from New York, “when I hear of people who are ill treated in one section of the country emigrating to another. Men who are deaf to the claims of mercy, and oblivious to the demands of justice, can feel when money is slipping from their pockets.”
“The negro,” said Hon. Dugdale, “does not present to my mind the picture of an effete and exhausted people, destined to die out before a stronger race. Gilbert Haven once saw a statue which suggested this thought, ‘I am black, but comely; the sun has looked down upon me, but I will teach you who despise me to feel that I am your superior.’ The men who are acquiring property and building up homes in the South show us what energy and determination may do even in that part of the country. I believe such men can do more to conquer prejudice than if they spent all their lives in shouting for their rights and ignoring their duties. No! as there are millions of us in this country, I think it best to settle down and work out our own salvation here.”

===Chinese===
“气候、土壤和自然环境，”格拉德诺教授说，“对民族性格的塑造大有影响。倘若黑人在非洲的热带烈日之下，在文明的行进中落在了其他种族后面，那么在这个国家里，他的历史上至少破天荒头一遭，得了利用气候之利的特权，得以在全新的条件下发展。”

“是啊，”拉蒂默医生答道，“而且我不希望我们的人民连一代人的自由都还没有体验过，就先变得心浮气躁、居无定所。”

“每逢听说在一地受尽虐待的人们迁往另一地，”来自纽约的高个子、气宇不凡的绅士福雷斯特先生（Mr. Forest）说，“我总是高兴的。对慈悲的呼吁充耳不闻、对正义的要求漠然不觉的人，等到钱从口袋里往外滑的时候，倒是感觉得到的。”

“在我心目中，”达格代尔先生（Hon. Dugdale）说，“黑人并不是一幅衰朽枯竭、注定要在更强种族面前灭绝的民族的图景。吉尔伯特·哈文（Gilbert Haven）曾见过一座雕像，那雕像令人生出这样的意念：‘我虽然黑，却是秀美；太阳曾低头照我，我却要教你们这些藐视我的人感受到：我比你们更高一等。’在南方购置产业、建立家园的人们，向我们显示了干劲与决心即便在那个地方也能有何等作为。我相信，这样的人比起把一辈子都耗在高喊自己的权利、无视自己的义务上，更能战胜偏见。不！我们在这个国家有数以百万计的人，我认为最好还是安顿下来，在这里成就我们自己的拯救。”

===Original===
“How many of us today,” asked Professor Langhorne, “would be teaching in the South, if every field of labor in the North was as accessible to us as to the whites? It has been estimated that a million young white men have left the South since the war, and, had our chances been equal to theirs, would we have been any more willing to stay in the South with those who need us than they? But this prejudice, by impacting us together, gives us a common cause and brings our intellect in contact with the less favored of our race.”
“I do not believe,” said Miss Delany, “that the Southern white people themselves desire any wholesale exodus of the colored from their labor fields. It would be suicidal to attempt their expatriation.”
“History,” said Professor Langhorne, “tells that Spain was once the place where barbarian Europe came to light her lamp. Seven hundred years before there was a public lamp in London you might have gone through the streets of Cordova amid ten miles of lighted lamps, and stood there on solidly paved land, when hundreds of years afterwards, in Paris, on a rainy day you would have sunk to your ankles in the mud. But she who bore the name of the ‘Terror of Nations,’ and the ‘Queen of the Ocean,’ was not strong enough to dash herself against God’s law of retribution and escape unscathed. She inaugurated a crusade of horror against a million of her best laborers and artisans. Vainly she expected the blessing of God to crown her work of violence. Instead of seeing the fruition of her hopes in the increased prosperity of her land, depression and paralysis settled on her trade and business. A fearful blow was struck at her agriculture; decay settled on her manufactories; money became too scarce to pay the necessary expenses of the king’s exchequer; and that once mighty empire became a fallen kingdom, pierced by her crimes and dragged down by her transgressions.”
“We did not,” said Iola, “place the bounds of our habitation. And I believe we are to be fixtures in this country. But beyond the shadows I see the coruscation of a brighter day; and we can help usher it in, not by answering hate with hate, or giving scorn for scorn, but by striving to be more generous, noble, and just. It seems as if all creation travels to respond to the song of the Herald angels, ‘Peace on earth, goodwill toward men.’ ”

===Chinese===
“今天，倘若北方的每一行营生都像向白人那样向我们敞开，”朗霍恩教授问道，“我们当中还会有多少人留在南方执教？据估计，战后已有百万白人青年离开了南方；倘若我们的机会与他们均等，我们难道会比他们更甘愿留在南方，留在那些需要我们的人身边吗？不过，这偏见把我们挤压到一处，反倒给了我们一项共同的事业，使我们的才智得与种族中际遇较次的同胞相接触。”

“我不认为，”德拉尼小姐说，“南方白人自己当真盼着有色人种成群结队地离开他们的劳作田野。想把这些人放逐出境，无异于自取灭亡。”

“历史告诉我们，”朗霍恩教授说，“西班牙曾是蛮荒的欧洲前来点亮自己灯火的地方。伦敦还没有一盏公共路灯的七百年前，你就可以穿行在科尔多瓦（Cordova）十里灯河的通衢之上，站在铺砌坚实的路面上；而数百年之后，在巴黎，逢着雨天，你却会陷进没踝的泥泞。可是，那个曾顶着‘万国震怖’与‘海洋女王’之名的国度，一头撞在上帝报应的法则上，还想全身而退，终究没有那样的力气。她对自己百万最优秀的劳工与匠人掀起了一场骇人的讨伐。她徒然指望上帝的祝福来为她那暴行加冕。她没有盼到国土日益繁荣、宿愿开花结果，萧条与瘫痪反倒降临在她的商业贸易之上。她的农业遭到可怕的一击；制造业陷入衰败；钱币稀缺得连王室国库的必要开销都难以应付；那曾经强盛的帝国终归沦为倾颓的王国，被她的罪行刺穿，被她的罪过拖垮。”

“我们居住的疆界，”伊奥拉说，“并不是我们自己划定的。而我相信，我们注定要在这个国家落地生根。但在重重阴影之外，我看见一个更光明的日子正在闪耀；我们能帮助迎来那个日子——不是以仇恨回应仇恨、以轻蔑回报轻蔑，而是奋力使自己变得更宽厚、更高尚、更公正。仿佛天地万物都在奔走，要应和传报天使的歌声：‘地上平安，人间善意。’”


===Original===
The next paper was on “Patriotism,” by Rev. Cantnor. It was a paper in which the white man was extolled as the master race, and spoke as if it were a privilege for the colored man to be linked to his destiny and to live beneath the shadow of his power. He asserted that the white race of this country is the broadest, most Christian, and humane of that branch of the human family.
Dr. Latimer took exception to his position. “Law,” he said, “is the pivot on which the whole universe turns; and obedience to law is the gauge by which a nation’s strength or weakness is tried. We have had two evils by which our obedience to law has been tested﻿—slavery and the liquor traffic. How have we dealt with them both? We have been weighed in the balance and found wanting. Millions of slaves and serfs have been liberated during this century, but not even in semi-barbaric Russia, heathen Japan, or Catholic Spain has slavery been abolished through such a fearful conflict as it was in the United States. The liquor traffic still sends its floods of ruin and shame to the habitations of men, and no political party has been found with enough moral power and numerical strength to stay the tide of death.”
“I think,” said Professor Gradnor, “that what our country needs is truth more than flattery. I do not think that our moral life keeps pace with our mental development and material progress. I know of no civilized country on the globe, Catholic, Protestant, or Mohammedan, where life is less secure than it is in the South. Nearly eighteen hundred years ago the life of a Roman citizen in Palestine was in danger from mob violence. That pagan government threw around him a wall of living clay, consisting of four hundred and seventy men, when more than forty Jews had bound themselves with an oath that they would neither eat nor drink until they had taken the life of the Apostle Paul. Does not true patriotism demand that citizenship should be as much protected in Christian America as it was in heathen Rome?”

===Chinese===
下一篇论文题为《爱国主义》，作者是坎特诺牧师（Rev. Cantnor）。那是一篇把白人推崇为主宰种族的文章，说得仿佛有色人种能与白人的命运相连结、活在白人权力的荫庇之下，便是一种特权似的。他断言，这个国家的白种人，是人类大家庭那一支系中胸怀最宽广、最富基督精神、也最仁厚的。

拉蒂默医生不赞成他的立场。“法律，”他说，“是整个宇宙赖以旋转的枢轴；对法律的服从，是检验一个民族是强是弱的准绳。我们经历过两样祸患，考验过我们对法律的服从——奴隶制与酒类买卖。对这两样，我们都是如何处置的？我们被放在天平上称量，显出了亏欠。本世纪已有千百万奴隶与农奴得到解放，可是无论是半开化的俄国、异教的日本，还是天主教的西班牙，奴隶制的废除都不曾像在美国这样，要经过一场如此可怕的冲突。酒类买卖至今仍把毁灭与耻辱的洪流灌进千家万户，而迄今还没有一个政党，具有足够的道义力量和人数实力，能遏止这股死亡的潮水。”

“依我看，”格拉德诺教授说，“我们这个国家需要的是真话，而不是奉承。我不认为我们的道德生活跟得上我们心智的发展与物质的进步。普天之下，天主教的、新教的、伊斯兰教的文明国家，我还不知道有哪一个，生命比在我们南方更缺乏保障。将近一千八百年前，巴勒斯坦一名罗马公民的性命，就曾险丧于暴民之手。那个异教政府却调来四百七十名兵士，给他围起一道血肉之墙——当时有四十多名犹太人起誓立愿，不取使徒保罗的性命，就绝不吃喝。难道真正的爱国主义，不该要求公民身份在基督教的美国得到不亚于异教罗马的保护吗？”

===Original===
“I would have our people,” said Miss Delany, “more interested in politics. Instead of forgetting the past, I would have them hold in everlasting remembrance our great deliverance. Hitherto we have never had a country with tender, precious memories to fill our eyes with tears, or glad reminiscences to thrill our hearts with pride and joy. We have been aliens and outcasts in the land of our birth. But I want my pupils to do all in their power to make this country worthy of their deepest devotion and loftiest patriotism. I want them to feel that its glory is their glory, its dishonor their shame.”
“Our esteemed friend, Mrs. Watson,” said Iola, “sends regrets that she cannot come, but has kindly favored us with a poem, called the ‘Rallying Cry.’ In her letter she says that, although she is no longer young, she feels that in the conflict for the right there’s room for young as well as old. She hopes that we will here unite the enthusiasm of youth with the experience of age, and that we will have a pleasant and profitable conference. Is it your pleasure that the poem be read at this stage of our proceedings, or later on?”
“Let us have it now,” answered Harry, “and I move that Miss Delany be chosen to lend to the poem the charm of her voice.”
“I second the motion,” said Iola, smiling, and handing the poem to Miss Delany.
Miss Delany took the poem and read it with fine effect. The spirit of the poem had entered her soul.

===Chinese===
“我倒希望我们的人民，”德拉尼小姐说，“更关心政治。我不愿他们忘掉过去，只愿他们把我们的那场伟大拯救永志不忘。直到今天，我们从来不曾有过一个祖国，能用温存珍贵的记忆叫我们热泪盈眶，或用欢欣的往事叫我们的心因自豪与喜悦而震颤。在我们生身的土地上，我们一直是异乡人与弃儿。可我要我的学生们竭尽所能，使这个国家配得上他们最深的挚爱与最崇高的爱国心。我要他们感到：她的光荣就是他们的光荣，她的耻辱就是他们的耻辱。”

“我们敬爱的朋友沃森太太（Mrs. Watson），”伊奥拉说，“因不能前来深致歉意，但承她厚意，惠赐了一首诗，题为《进军号》。她在信中说，自己虽已不再年轻，却觉得在为正义而战的斗争中，年轻人有立足之地，年老人也有立足之地。她希望我们今晚在此把青春的热忱与老成的阅历结合起来，开一个既愉快又有益的恳谈会。请诸位示下：这首诗是在议程的这个环节宣读，还是留待稍后？”

“这就读吧，”哈里答道，“我提议请德拉尼小姐来念，让她嗓音的魅力为这首诗增色。”

“我附议。”伊奥拉微笑着说，把诗稿递给德拉尼小姐。

德拉尼小姐接过诗稿，诵读得声情并茂。诗的神髓已经进入了她的灵魂。


===Original===
> Oh, children of the tropics,
 

 Amid our pain and wrong
 

 Have you no other mission
 

 Than music, dance, and song?
> When through the weary ages
 

 Our dripping tears still fall,
 

 Is this a time to dally
 

 With pleasure’s silken thrall?
> Go, muffle all your viols;
 

 As heroes learn to stand,
 

 With faith in God’s great justice
 

 Nerve every heart and hand.
> Dream not of ease nor pleasure,
 

 Nor honor, wealth, nor fame,
 

 Till from the dust you’ve lifted
 

 Our long-dishonored name;
> And crowned that name with glory
 

 By deeds of holy worth,
 

 To shine with light emblazoned,
 

 The noblest name on earth.
> Count life a dismal failure,
 

 Unblessing and unblest,
 

 That seeks ’mid ease inglorious
 

 For pleasure or for rest.
> With courage, strength, and valor
 

 Your lives and actions brace;
 

 Shrink not from toil or hardship,
 

 And dangers bravely face.
> Engrave upon your banners,
 

 In words of golden light,
 

 That honor, truth, and justice
 

 Are more than godless might.
> Above earth’s pain and sorrow
 

 Christ’s dying face I see;
 

 I hear the cry of anguish:﻿—
 

 “Why hast thou forsaken me?”
> In the pallor of that anguish
 

 I see the only light,
 

 To flood with peace and gladness
 

 Earth’s sorrow, pain, and night.
> Arrayed in Christly armor
 

 ’Gainst error, crime, and sin,
 

 The victory can’t be doubtful,
 

 For God is sure to win.

===Chinese===
> 哦，热带的儿女们，
 

 在我们的苦难与冤屈里，
 

 难道你们没有别的使命，
 

 只剩音乐、舞蹈与歌声？
> 当疲惫的世代悠悠，
 

 我们的泪珠兀自滴垂，
 

 这岂是嬉戏的时候，
 

 与逸乐的丝笼厮磨？
> 去吧，把你们的琴弦全都蒙起；
 

 像英雄那样挺身站立，
 

 怀着对上帝大公义的信心，
 

 振作每一颗心、每一只手。
> 莫梦想安逸与逸乐，
 

 莫梦想尊荣、财富、声名，
 

 直到你们从尘土中
 

 扶起我们久蒙羞辱的名声；
> 再以圣洁可贵的功业，
 

 为这名声戴上荣光之冠，
 

 让它披上辉煌的火光，
 

 成为地上最高贵的名字。
> 那样的一生，要算作
 

 凄惨的失败：不施福，不蒙福——
 

 在不光彩的安逸里
 

 只知追逐享乐与安息。
> 以勇气、力量与胆气，
 

 把你们的人生与作为撑起；
 

 劳苦艰辛莫退缩，
 

 危险临头勇敢面对。
> 在你们的旗帜上，
 

 用金色光明的字样镌明：
 

 荣誉、真理与正义，
 

 胜过目无上帝的强权。
> 越过尘世的痛苦忧伤，
 

 我望见基督临终的面容；
 

 我听见那声哀号：——
 

 “你为什么离弃我？”
> 在那苦痛的惨白之中，
 

 我看见唯一的光，
 

 要叫平安与欢欣
 

 漫过尘世的忧愁、伤痛与黑夜。
> 披上基督的甲胄，
 

 抵御谬误、罪行与罪孽，
 

 胜利必无可置疑，
 

 因为上帝必操胜算。


===Original===
The next paper was by Miss Iola Leroy, on the “Education of Mothers.”
“I agree,” said Rev. Eustace, of St. Mary’s parish, “with the paper. The great need of the race is enlightened mothers.”
“And enlightened fathers, too,” added Miss Delany, quickly. “If there is anything I chafe to see it is a strong, hearty man shirking his burdens, putting them on the shoulders of his wife, and taking life easy for himself.”
“I always pity such mothers,” interposed Iola, tenderly.
“I think,” said Miss Delany, with a flash in her eye and a ring of decision in her voice, “that such men ought to be drummed out of town!” As she spoke, there was an expression which seemed to say, “And I would like to help do it!”
Harry smiled, and gave her a quick glance of admiration.

===Chinese===
下一篇论文出自伊奥拉·勒罗伊小姐之手，题为《母亲的教育》。

“我赞同这篇论文，”圣玛丽教区（St. Mary’s parish）的尤斯塔斯牧师（Rev. Eustace）说，“这个种族最迫切的需要，是开明的母亲。”

“还得有开明的父亲。”德拉尼小姐赶忙补上一句。“若说有什么叫我看着憋气，那就是一个身强力壮的汉子逃避自己的担子，把担子撂到妻子肩上，自己倒把日子过得轻闲。”

“这样的母亲，我总是心疼。”伊奥拉柔声插话。

“依我看，”德拉尼小姐说，眼中闪过一道光，声音里带着决断的音响，“这样的男人，就该敲着鼓赶出城去！”说话间，她脸上有一种神情，仿佛在说：“我还想搭把手呢！”

哈里笑了，向她迅疾投去倾慕的一瞥。

===Original===
“I do not think,” said Mrs. Stillman, “that we can begin too early to teach our boys to be manly and self-respecting, and our girls to be useful and self-reliant.”
“You know,” said Mrs. Leroy, “that after the war we were thrown upon the nation a homeless race to be gathered into homes, and a legally unmarried race to be taught the sacredness of the marriage relation. We must instill into our young people that the true strength of a race means purity in women and uprightness in men; who can say, with Sir Galahad:﻿—

> ‘My strength is the strength of ten,
 

 Because my heart is pure.’

And where this is wanting neither wealth nor culture can make up the deficiency.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”斯蒂尔曼太太说，“教导我们的男孩要刚毅自尊、女孩要有用自立，再早也不嫌早。”

“你们都知道，”勒罗伊太太说，“战后，我们作为一个无家可归的种族被抛给了这个国家，有待收拢进一个个家庭；作为一个在法律上不算成婚的种族，有待受教婚姻关系的神圣。我们必须谆谆教导我们的年轻人：一个种族真正的力量，意味着女性的贞洁、男性的正直；唯有这样的人，才能同加拉哈德爵士（Sir Galahad）一起说：——

> ‘我的力量是十个人的力量，
 

 因为我的心纯洁。’

缺了这一条，无论财富还是教养，都补不上这个欠缺。”


===Original===
“There is a field of Christian endeavor which lies between the schoolhouse and the pulpit, which needs the hand of a woman more in private than in public,” said Miss Delany.
“Yes, I have often felt the need of such work in my own parish. We need a union of women with the warmest hearts and clearest brains to help in the moral education of the race,” said Rev. Eustace.
“Yes,” said Iola, “if we would have the prisons empty we must make the homes more attractive.”
“In civilized society,” replied Dr. Latimer, “there must be restraint either within or without. If parents fail to teach restraint within, society has her checkreins without in the form of chain-gangs, prisons, and the gallows.”
The closing paper was on the “Moral Progress of the Race,” by Hon. Dugdale. He said: “The moral progress of the race was not all he could desire, yet he could not help feeling that, compared with other races, the outlook was not hopeless. I am so sorry to see, however, that in some States there is an undue proportion of colored people in prisons.”
“I think,” answered Professor Langhorne, of Georgia, “that this is owing to a partial administration of law in meting out punishment to colored offenders. I know red-handed murderers who walk in this Republic unwhipped of justice, and I have seen a colored woman sentenced to prison for weeks for stealing twenty-five cents. I knew a colored girl who was executed for murder when only a child in years. And it was through the intervention of a friend of mine, one of the bravest young men of the South, that a boy of fifteen was saved from the gallows.”

===Chinese===
“在校舍与讲坛之间，有一片基督徒事奉的园地，”德拉尼小姐说，“这片园地需要女人的手，私下里的操持，多于公开场合的抛头露面。”

“是啊，我在自己的教区里就常常感到需要这样的工作。我们需要一个由心地最热、头脑最清的妇女组成的联合会，来协助这个种族的道德教育。”尤斯塔斯牧师说。

“是啊，”伊奥拉说，“倘若我们想要监狱空空荡荡，就得让家更招人爱恋。”

“在文明社会里，”拉蒂默医生答道，“约束不发自内，便施加于外。做父母的若没有教会子女内在的约束，社会自有她外在的缰绳，那就是锁链苦役队、监狱和绞刑架。”

最后一篇论文题为《种族的道德进步》，作者是达格代尔先生。他说：“这个种族的道德进步未尽如他所愿，但他仍不免感到，与其他种族相比，前景并非全然无望。只是他很痛心地看到，在一些州，监狱里有色人种所占的比例高得失常。”

“我认为，”佐治亚的朗霍恩教授答道，“这要归咎于法律在惩处有色人种罪犯时的偏私不公。我认识血债累累的杀人凶手，在这个共和国里逍遥法外、未受正义的鞭笞；我也亲眼见过一个有色人种妇女，只因偷了两角五分钱，就被判了几个星期的监禁。我认识一个有色人种姑娘，因杀人被处决时，论年纪还不过是个孩子。还有一个十五岁的男孩能免上绞架，靠的是我的一位朋友——南方最勇敢的青年之一——出面斡旋。”

===Original===
“When I look,” said Mr. Forest, “at the slow growth of modern civilization﻿—the ages which have been consumed in reaching our present altitude, and see how we have outgrown slavery, feudalism, and religious persecutions, I cannot despair of the future of the race.”
“Just now,” said Dr. Latimer, “we have the fearful grinding and friction which comes in the course of an adjustment of the new machinery of freedom in the old ruts of slavery. But I am optimistic enough to believe that there will yet be a far higher and better Christian civilization than our country has ever known.”
“And in that civilization I believe the negro is to be an important factor,” said Rev. Cantnor.
“I believe it also,” said Miss Delany, hopefully, “and this thought has been a blessed inspiration to my life. When I come in contact with Christless prejudices, I feel that my life is too much a part of the Divine plan, and invested with too much intrinsic worth, for me to be the least humiliated by indignities that beggarly souls can inflict. I feel more pitiful than resentful to those who do not know how much they miss by living mean, ignoble lives.”

===Chinese===
“当我放眼现代文明缓慢的生长——为了抵达今天的高度，多少岁月已被耗尽——又看到我们已经把奴隶制、封建制与宗教迫害远远甩在身后，”福雷斯特先生说，“我对这个种族的未来，就无法绝望。”

“眼下，”拉蒂默医生说，“我们正经历那可怕的碾压与摩擦——那是自由的新机件嵌进奴隶制的旧辙时必然出现的磨合。但我仍足够乐观，相信将来必有一种远比我国历来所知的更高尚、更美好的基督教文明。”

“而且我相信，在那文明之中，黑人将是一个重要的因素。”坎特诺牧师说。

“我也这样相信，”德拉尼小姐满怀希望地说，“这个念头一直是我生命中一种蒙福的激励。每当遭逢那些没有基督的偏见，我就感到：我的生命与神的计划相连太深，又赋有太重的内在价值，那些卑污的灵魂所能施加的凌辱，根本不能使我生出半点屈辱之感。对那些人，我与其怨恨，不如怜悯——他们不知道，活在卑劣、不名誉的生活里，自己错过了多少。”


===Original===
“My heart,” said Iola, “is full of hope for the future. Pain and suffering are the crucibles out of which come gold more fine than the pavements of heaven, and gems more precious than the foundations of the Holy City.”
“If,” said Mrs. Leroy, “pain and suffering are factors in human development, surely we have not been counted too worthless to suffer.”
“And is there,” continued Iola, “a path which we have trodden in this country, unless it be the path of sin, into which Jesus Christ has not put His feet and left it luminous with the light of His steps? Has the negro been poor and homeless? The birds of the air had nests and the foxes had holes, but the Son of man had not where to lay His head. Has our name been a synonym for contempt? ‘He shall be called a Nazarene.’ Have we been despised and trodden under foot? Christ was despised and rejected of men. Have we been ignorant and unlearned? It was said of Jesus Christ, ‘How knoweth this man letters, never having learned?’ Have we been beaten and bruised in the prison-house of bondage? ‘They took Jesus and scourged Him.’ Have we been slaughtered, our bones scattered at the graves’ mouth? He was spit upon by the mob, smitten and mocked by the rabble, and died as died Rome’s meanest criminal slave. Today that cross of shame is a throne of power. Those robes of scorn have changed to habiliments of light, and that crown of mockery to a diadem of glory. And never, while the agony of Gethsemane and the sufferings of Calvary have their hold upon my heart, will I recognize any religion as His which despises the least of His brethren.”

===Chinese===
“我的心，”伊奥拉说，“对未来充满希望。痛苦与磨难是一座座熔炉，从其中炼出的黄金，比天城的街道更纯；炼出的宝石，比圣城的根基更贵。”

“倘若，”勒罗伊太太说，“痛苦与磨难真是人类发展的要素，那么可以断言，我们还没有被看得卑贱到连受苦的资格都没有。”

“再说，”伊奥拉接着说，“在这个国家，除了罪孽之路，可还有哪一条我们踏过的路，是耶稣基督不曾把自己的脚放上去、不曾用他脚步的光照亮过的？黑人贫穷无家吗？天空的飞鸟有窝，狐狸有洞，人子却没有枕头的地方。我们的名字成了轻蔑的同义语吗？‘他必称为拿撒勒人。’我们受尽藐视、被人踩在脚下吗？基督也曾被藐视、被人厌弃。我们蒙昧无知、没有学问吗？人们论到耶稣基督曾问：‘这个人没有学过，怎么明白书呢？’我们在奴役的牢狱里被打得遍体鳞伤吗？‘他们拿住耶稣，鞭打他。’我们惨遭屠戮、骸骨抛散在墓口吗？他被暴民唾弃，被乌合之众击打、戏弄，死得像罗马最低贱的罪犯奴隶一样。今天，那羞辱的十字架已成权能的宝座；那些嘲弄的衣袍已化作光明的华服；那戏弄的冠冕已换成荣耀的冕旒。只要客西马尼的痛楚与各各他的苦难还攫着我的心，我就永不承认那藐视他至微小的弟兄的宗教，是他的宗教。”

===Original===
As Iola finished, there was a ring of triumph in her voice, as if she were reviewing a path she had trodden with bleeding feet, and seen it change to lines of living light. Her soul seemed to be flashing through the rare loveliness of her face and etherealizing its beauty.
Everyone was spellbound. Dr. Latimer was entranced, and, turning to Hon. Dugdale, said, in a low voice and with deep-drawn breath, “She is angelic!”
Hon. Dugdale turned, gave a questioning look, then replied, “She is strangely beautiful! Do you know her?”
“Yes; I have met her several times. I accompanied her here tonight. The tones of her voice are like benedictions of peace; her words a call to higher service and nobler life.”

===Chinese===
伊奥拉说完时，声音里回荡着一种胜利的音响，仿佛她正回望一条自己曾淌着血走下的路，又眼看它化作一道道流动的光。她的灵魂仿佛正透过那张罕有的秀美面庞灼灼闪现，使那份美更显空灵。

人人都听得出了神。拉蒂默医生心醉神驰，转向达格代尔先生，压低了嗓音、深深吸了一口气说：“她真是天使一般的人！”

达格代尔先生转过脸来，投去询问的一瞥，答道：“她美得出奇！你认识她吗？”

“认识；我同她见过好几面。今晚就是陪她一起来的。她声音里的音调，像一声声赐人平安的祝福；她的话语，是对更高的事奉、更高的人生的召唤。”


===Original===
Just then Rev. Carmicle was announced. He had been on a Southern tour, and had just returned.
“Oh, Doctor,” exclaimed Mrs. Stillman, “I am delighted to see you. We were about to adjourn, but we will postpone action to hear from you.”
“Thank you,” replied Rev. Carmicle. “I have not the cue to the meeting, and will listen while I take breath.”
“Pardon me,” answered Mrs. Stillman. “I should have been more thoughtful than to press so welcome a guest into service before I had given him time for rest and refreshment; but if the courtesy failed on my lips it did not fail in my heart. I wanted our young folks to see one of our thinkers who had won distinction before the war.”
“My dear friend,” said Rev. Carmicle, smiling, “some of these young folks will look on me as a back number. You know the cry has already gone forth, ‘Young men to the front.’ ”

===Chinese===
正在这时，有人通报卡迈克牧师到了。他刚游历南方归来。

“哦，博士，见到您真叫人高兴。我们正要散会，不过散会的事且搁一搁，先听听您的高见。”斯蒂尔曼太太欢声说道。

“谢谢您，”卡迈克牧师答道，“今晚聚会的头绪我还没摸着，且让我一边歇口气，一边听。”

“恕我失礼，”斯蒂尔曼太太答道，“我原该更体贴些，不该这样把一位如此受欢迎的贵客，连歇息、用点茶点的工夫都不给，就拉来效劳；只是这份礼数若在我唇上有所欠缺，在我心里却未曾欠缺。我原想让我们的年轻人见一见我们的一位思想家——一位战前就已赢得声望的人。”

“亲爱的朋友，”卡迈克牧师含笑说，“这些年轻人里，怕是有些要把我看成过时的人物了。你知道，那呼声已经喊出去了：‘年轻人，到前头来。’”

===Original===
“But we need old men for counsel,” interposed Mr. Forest, of New York.
“Of course,” said Rev. Carmicle, “we older men would rather retire gracefully than be relegated or hustled to a back seat. But I am pleased to see doors open to you which were closed to us, and opportunities which were denied us embraced by you.”
“How,” asked Hon. Dugdale, “do you feel in reference to our people’s condition in the South?”
“Very hopeful, although at times I cannot help feeling anxious about their future. I was delighted with my visits to various institutions of learning, and surprised at the desire manifested among the young people to obtain an education. Where toil-worn mothers bent beneath their heavy burdens their more favored daughters are enjoying the privileges of education. Young people are making recitations in Greek and Latin where it was once a crime to teach their parents to read. I also became acquainted with colored professors and presidents of colleges. Saw young ladies who had graduated as doctors. Comfortable homes have succeeded old cabins of slavery. Vast crops have been raised by free labor. I read with interest and pleasure a number of papers edited by colored men. I saw it estimated that two millions of our people had learned to read, and I feel deeply grateful to the people who have supplied us with teachers who have stood their ground so nobly among our people.”

===Chinese===
“可是出谋献策，还少不了老年人呢。”纽约的福雷斯特先生插话道。

“那是自然，”卡迈克牧师说，“我们这些上了年纪的人，宁愿体面地隐退，也不愿被人打发到、挤到后排的座位上去。不过我很高兴看到，对我们关着的门向你们敞开了，我们被剥夺的机会被你们紧紧抓住了。”

“南方我们同胞的境况，”达格代尔先生问道，“您感受如何？”

“很有希望，虽说有时我也不免为他们的前途悬心。我走访了几所学府，兴致极浓；年轻人身上表现出的求学渴望，令我惊讶。当年操劳的母亲们被重担压弯了腰的地方，如今她们境遇较好的女儿们正享受着受教育的权利。年轻人正在朗诵希腊文、拉丁文，而就在那同一片地方，当年教他们的父母认字还是一桩罪过。我还结识了一些有色人种的教授和学院院长。见过以医生身份毕业的年轻女士。舒适的家园接替了奴隶制的旧木屋。自由劳动种出了大片大片的庄稼。我怀着兴味与快意读了好几份由有色人种主编的报纸。我看到有人估计，我们的人民中已有两百万人学会了读书写字；对于那些给我们派来教师的人们——他们能在我们的人民中间如此高风亮节地坚守岗位——我由衷感激。”


===Original===
“But,” asked Mr. Forest, “you expressed fears about the future of our race. From whence do your fears arise?”
“From the unfortunate conditions which slavery has entailed upon that section of our country. I dread the results of that racial feeling which ever and anon breaks out into restlessness and crime. Also, I am concerned about the lack of home training for those for whom the discipline of the plantation has been exchanged for the penalties of prisons and chain-gangs. I am sorry to see numbers of our young men growing away from the influence of the church and drifting into prisons. I also fear that in some sections, as colored men increase in wealth and intelligence, there will be an increase of race rivalry and jealousy. It is said that savages, by putting their ears to the ground, can hear a far-off tread. So, today, I fear that there are savage elements in our civilization which hear the advancing tread of the negro and would retard his coming. It is the incarnation of these elements that I dread. It is their elimination I do so earnestly desire. Whether it be outgrown or not is our unsolved problem. Time alone will tell whether or not the virus of slavery and injustice has too fully permeated our Southern civilization for a complete recovery. Nations, honeycombed by vice, have fallen beneath the weight of their iniquities. Justice is always uncompromising in its claims and inexorable in its demands. The laws of the universe are never repealed to accommodate our follies.”
“Surely,” said Bishop Tunster, “the negro has a higher mission than that of aimlessly drifting through life and patiently waiting for death.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”福雷斯特先生问道，“您方才对我们的种族前途表示过忧虑。您的忧虑从何而来呢？”

“来自奴隶制给我们国家那一方土地遗留下来的种种不幸境况。我畏惧那种种族情绪的后果——它动不动就爆发成骚乱与暴行。我也挂念那些孩子：种植园的管教换成了监狱与锁链苦役队的刑罚之后，他们便失了家庭的训导。看到我们为数不少的年轻人日渐脱离教会的影响、漂进监狱，我心里难过。我还担心，在某些地区，随着有色人种财富与智识的增长，种族间的角逐与嫉恨也会随之增长。据说野蛮人把耳朵贴在地上，就能听见远处的脚步声。所以今天，我担心我们的文明里也有一些野蛮的成分，听见了黑人日益逼近的脚步声，便要挡住他的来路。我所畏惧的，正是这些成分结胎成形；我如此恳切盼望的，正是把它们清除净尽。这个问题能否随着时代成长而消解，是我们尚未解开的难题。奴隶制与不公的病毒，是否已把我们的南方文明浸透得太深、深到无法完全康复，唯有时间能作分晓。被罪恶蛀成蜂窝一般的国家，都在自己罪孽的重压下倾覆了。正义的主张从不妥协，正义的要求从不留情。宇宙的法则从不曾为了迁就我们的愚行而废止。”

“黑人的使命，”滕斯特主教说，“确乎高于浑浑噩噩地虚度一生、耐心地坐待死亡。”

===Original===
“We may not,” answered Rev. Carmicle, “have the same dash, courage, and aggressiveness of other races, accustomed to struggle, achievement, and dominion, but surely the world needs something better than the results of arrogance, aggressiveness, and indomitable power. For the evils of society there are no solvents as potent as love and justice, and our greatest need is not more wealth and learning, but a religion replete with life and glowing with love. Let this be the impelling force in the race and it cannot fail to rise in the scale of character and condition.”
“And,” said Dr. Latimer, “instead of narrowing our sympathies to mere racial questions, let us broaden them to humanity’s wider issues.”
“Let us,” replied Rev. Carmicle, “pass it along the lines, that to be willfully ignorant is to be shamefully criminal. Let us teach our people not to love pleasure or to fear death, but to learn the true value of life, and to do their part to eliminate the paganism of caste from our holy religion and the lawlessness of savagery from our civilization.”

===Chinese===
“我们也许没有别的种族那股锐气、勇气与进取心——他们惯于斗争、建树与统治；可是这个世界确乎需要一些比傲慢、进逼与不折不挠的强权之所得更好的东西。要消解社会的弊病，没有哪剂药方比得上爱与正义；我们最大的需要，不是更多的财富与学问，而是一种充满生命、燃着爱火的宗教。让这成为推动这个种族前行的力量吧，那它在品格与境况的天平上，必然步步上升。”

“还有，”拉蒂默医生说，“与其把我们的同情收窄到区区种族问题上，不如把它放宽到人类更宏大的议题上去。”

“让我们把这句话沿着一列列队伍传下去：甘心无知，便是可耻的犯罪。”卡迈克牧师答道，“让我们教导我们的人民，不贪恋逸乐，也不惧怕死亡，而要认清生命的真正价值，并且各尽本分，把种姓的异教崇拜从我们神圣的宗教中剔除出去，把野蛮的目无法纪从我们的文明中剔除出去。”

---

===Original===
“How did you enjoy the evening, Marie?” asked Robert, as they walked homeward.
“I was interested and deeply pleased,” answered Marie.
“I,” said Robert, “was thinking of the wonderful changes that have come to us since the war. When I sat in those well-lighted, beautifully-furnished rooms, I was thinking of the meetings we used to have in bygone days. How we used to go by stealth into lonely woods and gloomy swamps, to tell of our hopes and fears, sorrows and trials. I hope that we will have many more of these gatherings. Let us have the next one here.”
“I am sure,” said Marie, “I would gladly welcome such a conference at any time. I think such meetings would be so helpful to our young people.”

===Chinese===
“玛丽，今晚这个聚会，你觉得怎么样？”归途上，罗伯特问道。

“我听得很有兴味，心里也深深欢喜。”玛丽答道。

“我嘛，”罗伯特说，“一路在想战后降临到我们身上的种种奇妙变化。我坐在那些灯光明亮、陈设雅致的房间里时，就想起了往昔年月里我们的那些聚会。那时候，我们得蹑手蹑脚溜进僻静的树林、阴森的沼泽，去倾诉我们的希望与恐惧、悲伤与磨难。我盼着这样的聚会往后多多地办。下一次，就办在我们这儿吧。”

“说真的，”玛丽说，“这样的恳谈会，无论什么时候办，我都乐意迎来家中。我想，这样的聚会对我们的年轻人定然大有益处。”

## XXII Further Lifting of the Veil / 第二十二章 再度揭开面纱

===Original===
In one of those fearful conflicts by which the Mississippi was freed from Rebel intrusion and opened to commerce Harry was severely wounded, and forced to leave his place in the ranks for a bed in the hospital.

One day, as he lay in his bed, thinking of his former home in Mississippi and wondering if the chances of war would ever restore him to his loved ones, he fell into a quiet slumber. When he awoke he found a lady bending over him, holding in her hands some fruit and flowers. As she tenderly bent over Harry’s bed their eyes met, and with a thrill of gladness they recognized each other.

“Oh, my son, my son!” cried Marie, trying to repress her emotion, as she took his wasted hand in hers, and kissed the pale cheeks that sickness and suffering had blanched. Harry was very weak, but her presence was a call to life. He returned the pressure of her hand, kissed it, and his eyes grew full of sudden light, as he murmured faintly, but joyfully:﻿—

===Chinese===
密西西比河（Mississippi River）正是通过那一连串可怕的战事，才得以摆脱叛军的侵扰、重新向商旅开放；哈里（Harry）在这样一场恶战中身负重伤，被迫离开队列中的位置，躺进了医院的病床。

一天，他躺在床上，想念着密西西比旧日的家园，不知战事的机缘究竟还能不能把他送回亲人身边，想着想着便安然睡去。醒来时，他见一位女士正俯身望着他，手里捧着几样水果和鲜花。她温柔地俯在哈里的床边，两人的目光相遇，彼此认出了对方，心头霎时掠过一阵狂喜。

“哦，我的儿，我的儿！”玛丽（Marie）喊道，一面强自按捺着激动的情绪，一面把他那只瘦削的手握在自己手里，亲吻那被疾病和苦难褪尽了血色的苍白面颊。哈里虚弱极了，可她的到来，无异于对生命的一声召唤。他回握住她的手，吻了吻它，眼里骤然盈满光彩，微弱却满心欢喜地呢喃道：

===Original===
“Mamma; oh, mamma! have I found you at last?”

The effort was too much, and he immediately became unconscious.

Anxious, yet hopeful, Marie sat by the bedside of her son till consciousness was restored. Caressingly she bent over his couch, murmuring in her happiness the tenderest, sweetest words of motherly love. In Harry’s veins flowed new life and vigor, calming the restlessness of his nerves.

As soon as possible Harry was carried to his mother’s home; a home brought into the light of freedom by the victories of General Grant. Nursed by his mother’s tender, loving care, he rapidly recovered, but, being too disabled to reenter the army, he was honorably discharged.

===Chinese===
“妈妈；哦，妈妈！我终于找到你了吗？”

这一下用力太过了，他随即不省人事。

玛丽焦灼不安，却又满怀指望，守在儿子床边，直到他恢复知觉。她爱怜地俯在他的卧榻上，在幸福中低语着母爱里最温柔、最甜蜜的话语。哈里的血管里流进了新的生命与活力，平复了他神经的躁动不安。

一俟情况允许，哈里便被送进母亲的家；那是一座因格兰特（Grant）将军的赫赫战功而重见自由之光的家园。在母亲温柔慈爱的照护下，他迅速康复；只是伤残太重，无法重返军队，遂光荣退伍。

===Original===
Lorraine had taken Marie to Vicksburg, and there allowed her to engage in confectionery and preserving for the wealthy ladies of the city. He had at first attempted to refugee with her in Texas, but, being foiled in the attempt, he was compelled to enlist in the Confederate Army, and met his fate by being killed just before the surrender of Vicksburg.

“My dear son,” Marie would say, as she bent fondly over him, “I am deeply sorry that you are wounded, but I am glad that the fortunes of war have brought us together. Poor Iola! I *do* wonder what has become of her? Just as soon as this war is over I want you to search the country all over. Poor child! How my heart has ached for her!”

Time passed on. Harry and his mother searched and inquired for Iola, but no tidings of her reached them.

Having fully recovered his health, and seeing the great need of education for the colored people, Harry turned his attention toward them, and joined the new army of Northern teachers.

===Chinese===
洛兰（Lorraine）早先把玛丽带到了维克斯堡（Vicksburg），在那里许她靠替城里的阔太太们做糖果点心、腌蜜饯果酱营生。他起初本想带她逃往得克萨斯（Texas）避难，无奈未能走脱，只得应征入了叛军，在维克斯堡守军投降前夕被击毙，落了个命定的下场。

“我亲爱的儿，”玛丽总是慈爱地俯身向着他，说道，“你负了伤，我心里难过得很；可战争的机运让我们母子团圆，我又高兴。可怜的伊奥拉（Iola）！我真不知她如今落到了什么地步？只等这场战争一结束，我要你把全国各地寻个遍。可怜的孩子！我这份心，为她疼了多少回！”

时光流转。哈里和母亲四处寻访、四处打听伊奥拉的下落，却始终没有得到她的半点音讯。

哈里完全康复之后，眼见有色人种对教育的需求何等迫切，便把心力转向了他们，加入了北方教师组成的这支新军。

===Original===
He still continued his inquiries for his sister, not knowing whether or not she had succumbed to the cruel change in her life. He thought she might have passed into the white basis for the sake of bettering her fortunes. Hope deferred, which had sickened his mother’s heart, had only roused him to renewed diligence.

A school was offered him in Georgia, and thither he repaired, taking his mother with him. They were soon established in the city of A﻿—﻿—. In hope of finding Iola he visited all the conferences of the Methodist Church, but for a long time his search was in vain.

“Mamma,” said Harry, one day during his vacation, “there is to be a Methodist Conference in this State in the city of S﻿—﻿—, about one hundred and fifty miles from here. I intend to go and renew my search for Iola.”

“Poor child!” burst out Marie, as the tears gathered in her eyes, “I wonder if she is living.”

“I think so,” said Harry, kissing the pale cheek of his mother; “I don’t feel that Iola is dead. I believe we will find her before long.”

===Chinese===
他仍旧继续打听姐姐的下落，不知她可曾被命运那残酷的变故压垮。他想，她或许是为了改善境遇，已经挤进了白人的行列。所盼望的迟延未得，令他母亲的心忧苦成病；于他，却只是激发出加倍的勤勉。

佐治亚（Georgia）有所学校延聘他任教，他便带着母亲赴任去了。两人很快在 A—— 城安顿下来。为寻伊奥拉，他把卫理公会（Methodist Church）各地的年会走遍了，可很长时间里，他的寻访都归于徒劳。

“妈妈，”哈里有一天趁假期说道，“本州在 S—— 城要开一场卫理公会年会，离这儿大约一百五十英里。我打算去，把寻访伊奥拉的事再接着做起来。”

“可怜的孩子！”玛丽脱口喊道，泪水涌上她的眼睛，“也不知她还活不活在世上。”

“我想还活着，”哈里说着，吻了吻母亲苍白的脸颊；“我感觉到伊奥拉没有死。我相信不久我们就会找到她。”

===Original===
“It seems to me my heart would burst with joy to see my dear child just once more. I am glad that you are going. When will you leave?”

“Tomorrow morning.”

“Well, my son, go, and my prayers will go with you,” was Marie’s tender parting wish.

Early next morning Harry started for the conference, and reached the church before the morning session was over. Near him sat two ladies, one fair, the other considerably darker. There was something in the fairer one that reminded him forcibly of his sister, but she was much older and graver than he imagined his sister to be. Instantly he dismissed the thought that had forced itself into his mind, and began to listen attentively to the proceedings of the conference.

===Chinese===
“在我看来，只要还能再见我亲爱的孩子一面，我这颗心都要欢喜得炸开了。你要去，我很高兴。什么时候动身？”

“明天一早。”

“好吧，我的儿，去吧，我的祷告会一路随着你。”这便是玛丽温柔的临别心愿。

第二天一早，哈里便动身赴会，晨会尚未结束就到了教堂。他身旁坐着两位女士，一位肤色白皙，另一位则深得多。那位白皙的身上有某种东西，叫他不由得强烈地想起自己的姐姐；只是她比他想象中的姐姐苍老、凝重得多。他即刻把这硬闯进心头的念头挥开，专心听起大会的议程来。

===Original===
When the regular business of the morning session was over the bishop arose and said:﻿—

“I have an interesting duty to perform. I wish to introduce a young lady to the conference, who was the daughter of a Mississippi planter. She is now in search of her mother and brother, from whom she was sold a few months before the war. Her father married her mother in Ohio, where he had taken her to be educated. After his death they were robbed of their inheritance and enslaved by a distant relative named Lorraine. Miss Iola Leroy is the young lady’s name. If anyone can give the least information respecting the objects of her search it will be thankfully received.”

“I can,” exclaimed a young man, rising in the midst of the audience, and pressing eagerly, almost impetuously, forward. “I am her brother, and I came here to look for her.”

Iola raised her eyes to his face, so flushed and bright with the glow of recognition, rushed to him, threw her arms around his neck, kissed him again and again, crying: “O, Harry!” Then she fainted from excitement. The women gathered around her with expressions of tender sympathy, and gave her all the care she needed. They called her the “dear child,” for without any effort on her part she had slidden into their hearts and found a ready welcome in each sympathizing bosom.

Harry at once telegraphed the glad tidings to his mother, who waited their coming with joyful anticipation. Long before the cars reached the city, Mrs. Leroy was at the depot, restlessly walking the platform or eagerly peering into the darkness to catch the first glimpse of the train which was bearing her treasures.

===Chinese===
晨会的例行议程完毕后，主教站起身来，说道：

“我有一项有意思的职责要履行。我想向大会引见一位年轻女士，她原是一位密西西比种植园主的女儿。她如今正在寻访自己的母亲和弟弟——开战前几个月，她被人从他们身边卖掉了。她的父亲当年把她的母亲带到俄亥俄（Ohio）去受教育，就在那里娶了她为妻。父亲死后，她们被人夺去了应继承的家产，又被一个名叫洛兰的远房亲戚发还为奴。这位年轻女士，名叫伊奥拉·勒罗伊小姐。若有人能就她所要寻找的亲人提供哪怕最少的一点消息，她都将满怀感激地领受。”

“我能！”人群中间一个小伙子应声喊道，站起身来，热切地、几乎是情不自禁地往前挤。“我就是她的弟弟，我就是到这里来找她的。”

伊奥拉抬眼望着他的脸——那张脸因相认的狂喜涨得通红、焕发着光彩——便朝他奔过去，伸出双臂搂住他的脖子，一遍又一遍地吻他，口中喊道：“哦，哈里！”随即她激动得晕了过去。妇女们围拢到她身边，个个带着温柔的怜惜，给了她所需的悉心照料。她们唤她“亲爱的孩子”，因为她不曾费一分气力，就悄悄滑进了众人心里，在每一颗感同身受的胸怀中都得到了现成的欢迎。

哈里当即把这喜讯电告母亲，母亲便怀着欢欣的期盼等候他们回来。火车离城还早着呢，勒罗伊太太（Mrs. Leroy）就已到了车站，在月台上焦躁地踱来踱去，又急切地朝黑暗里张望，想第一眼望见那趟载着她的珍宝的列车。

===Original===
At length the cars arrived, and, as Harry and Iola alighted, Marie rushed forward, clasped Iola in her arms and sobbed out her joy in broken words.

Very happy was the little family that sat together around the supper-table for the first time for years. They partook of that supper with thankful hearts and with eyes overflowing with tears of joy. Very touching were the prayers the mother uttered, when she knelt with her children that night to return thanks for their happy reunion, and to seek protection through the slumbers of the night.

The next morning, as they sat at the breakfast-table, Marie said:

“My dear child, you are so changed I do not think I would have known you if I had met you in the street!”

===Chinese===
火车终于到了。哈里和伊奥拉一下车，玛丽便抢步迎上，把伊奥拉紧紧搂在怀里，泣不成声，用断断续续的话语倾吐她的欢喜。

这小小的一家子，多年来头一回围坐在晚餐桌旁，真是快活极了。他们怀着感恩的心共享那顿晚餐，眼里满溢着喜悦的泪水。当夜，母亲与儿女们一同跪下，为这快乐的团圆献上感恩，又祈求在夜的酣睡中蒙受护佑；她所献上的祷告，动人极了。

第二天早晨，一家人正坐在早餐桌旁，玛丽说道：

“亲爱的孩子，你变得太多啦！要是在街上遇见你，我怕是认不出你来！”

===Original===
“And I,” said Harry, “can hardly realize that you are our own Iola, whom I recognized as sister a half dozen years ago.”

“Am I so changed?” asked Iola, as a faint sigh escaped her lips.

“Why, Iola,” said Harry, “you used to be the most harum-scarum girl I ever knew, laughing, dancing, and singing from morning until night.”

“Yes, I remember,” said Iola. “It all comes back to me like a dream. Oh, mamma! I have passed through a fiery ordeal of suffering since then. But it is useless,” and as she continued her face assumed a brighter look, “to brood over the past. Let us be happy in the present. Let me tell you something which will please you. Do you remember telling me about your mother and brother?”

===Chinese===
“还有我，”哈里说，“也简直不敢相信，你就是我们自家的伊奥拉，就是我六年前认下的那位姐姐。”

“我变了这么多吗？”伊奥拉问，唇边逸出一声轻叹。

“哎呀，伊奥拉，”哈里说，“你从前可是我认得的最疯疯癫癫的姑娘，从早到晚又是笑、又是跳、又是唱。”

“是啊，我记得，”伊奥拉说。“这一切像梦一样回到我眼前。哦，妈妈！打那以后，我经历了一场烈火般的磨难。可是，”她接着说下去，脸上透出明亮些的神色，“一味对过去愁眉不展是没有用的。让我们为眼前的日子欢喜吧。我告诉你们一件事，保管你们听了高兴。你还记得你从前跟我说起过你的母亲和弟弟吗？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Marie, in a questioning tone.

“Well,” continued Iola, with eyes full of gladness, “I think I have found them.”

“Can it be possible!” exclaimed Marie, in astonishment. “It is more than thirty years since we parted. I fear you are mistaken.”

“No, mamma; I have drawn my conclusions from good circumstantial evidence. After I was taken from you, I passed through a fearful siege of suffering, which would only harrow up your soul to hear. I often shudder at the remembrance. The last man in whose clutches I found myself was mean, brutal, and cruel. I was in his power when the Union army came into C﻿—﻿—, where I was living. A number of colored men stampeded to the Union ranks, with a gentleman as a leader, whom I think is your brother. A friend of his reported my case to the commander of the post, who instantly gave orders for my release. A place was given me as nurse in the hospital. I attended that friend in his last illness. Poor fellow! he was the best friend I had in all the time I have been tossing about. The gentleman whom I think is your brother appeared to be very anxious about his friend’s recovery, and was deeply affected by his death.
